# TapeRescue Verification Log

Generated by `python scripts/collect_verification.py` from `verification/entries/*.md`, `verification/pending.md` and `verification/traceability.md`. Do not edit this file by hand.

## Task 1: Repository scaffold and verification log tooling

Requirements: 21.1, 22.1, 22.2, 22.3

### Changes

- `pyproject.toml`: `taperescue` package, `requires-python = ">=3.12"`, console entry point `taperescue = "taperescue.cli:main"`, dev extra with `pytest>=8` and `hypothesis>=6.100`, pytest configured with `--import-mode=importlib`.

## Task 2.1: Vendor AtomTapeUtils and write the build script
Requirements: 1.1, 1.3, 1.4
### Changes
- `third_party/AtomTapeUtils/`: the 146 tracked files of upstream commit `98d1a8d1bed00aa22f1d72e3d5e91a254a69ec64`, extracted with `git archive` (no `.git` directory), plus `REVISION` containing the full commit id.
- `scripts/build_decoder.sh` (executable): fetch only when the vendored tree is absent, verify the revision, copy to `build/decoder-src/`, `git apply --check` then `git apply`, build the `ScanTape` CMake target, copy the binary to `build/decoder/ScanTape`, and write `build/decoder/BUILD_INFO` (`revision=...`, `patch=none` or patch name and SHA-256).
### Upstream layout compared with the design
- The upstream license file is `LICENSE.txt`, not `LICENSE`. It is the MIT text with the template line `Copyright (c) [year] [fullname]` left unfilled upstream. It is kept byte for byte: SHA-256 `002c2696d92b5c8cf956c11072baa58eaf9f6ade995c031ea635c6a1ee342ad1` for both the upstream checkout and the vendored copy. Task 2.3 and the notices task should check `LICENSE.txt`.
- `gzstream/gzstream.h` and `gzstream/gzstream.cpp` carry their own LGPL-2.1-or-later header (Copyright (C) 2001 Deepak Bandyopadhyay, Lutz Kettner). These are retained unchanged. `ScanTape` links the `gzstream` static library, so `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` must list it. No other files contain copyright or license text.
- Build system: one top-level `CMakeLists.txt` (UTF-8 with BOM, `cmake_minimum_required 3.8`, C++17, `find_package(ZLIB REQUIRED)`) defining every tool, with `gzstream/` and `shared/` subdirectory libraries. There is no `ScanTape/CMakeLists.txt`. The script builds only `--target ScanTape`.
- Upstream sets `CMAKE_RUNTIME_OUTPUT_DIRECTORY` to `${CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR}/bin`, so the binary lands in `build/decoder-src/bin/ScanTape` and the script copies it to `build/decoder/ScanTape`. The CMake build tree is `build/decoder-build/`.
- All tracked files use LF line endings (`git ls-files --eol`), except `docs/audio.png` (binary). `.gitattributes` is `* text=auto`.
- The script runs `git init` inside `build/decoder-src/`. Without it, `git apply` run there would resolve patch paths against the enclosing TapeRescue repository and skip them as outside the current directory.
### Revision verification
- `REVISION` must equal the pinned commit (or `git rev-parse HEAD` when the vendored tree is a git checkout).
- In addition, the script computes the git tree id of the vendored directory (excluding `REVISION`) in a throwaway bare repository and requires `faf78c9b7ed3cf4a1cd2c2aaabc68716e8ec9f86`, the tree of the pinned commit (`git rev-parse 98d1a8d1...^{tree}`). Any edited, added, or removed file fails with exit 4.
- During vendoring, every vendored file's `git hash-object` was compared with the blob id in `git ls-tree -r` of the pinned commit: 0 mismatches.
### Patch handling
`patches/atomtapeutils-json-observations.patch` does not exist yet (task 2.2). If the patch file is missing, the script exits 6 unless `--unpatched` is given, so an unpatched binary cannot be built by accident once integration work depends on the patch.

Exit codes: 0 success, 1 usage or missing tool, 2 fetch failure, 3 patch does not apply, 4 revision mismatch, 5 build failure, 6 patch missing. Environment overrides for tests: `TAPERESCUE_ATU_URL`, `TAPERESCUE_ATU_VENDOR`, `TAPERESCUE_ATU_PATCH`, `TAPERESCUE_BUILD_DIR`.
### Toolchain
`g++ (Ubuntu 13.3.0-6ubuntu2~24.04.1) 13.3.0`, `cmake version 3.28.3`, `git version 2.43.0`, `zlib1g-dev 1:1.3.dfsg-3.1ubuntu2.3` (CMake found ZLIB 1.3 at `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libz.so`).

### Notes for task 2.2 (line numbers in the pinned `shared/` sources)
- `shared/BlockDecoder.cpp` (403 lines): `bool BlockDecoder::readBlock(BlockTiming blockTiming, bool firstBlock, FileBlock& readBlock, bool& leadToneDetected, BlockError& readStatus)` spans lines 86–356.
  - Lines 116–127: carrier wait (returns false at lines 121 and 126). The first BBC block uses `waitForCarrierWithDummyByte`, which expects prelude tone, then a dummy byte, then the lead tone. Return false here means `leadToneDetected == false`.
  - Lines 141–149: `0x2A` sync byte through `checkBytes` (return false at line 148). Lines 158–165: `getBlockName`, the name plus terminator, added to the CRC. Lines 167–179: rest of header through `mReader.readBytes(hdr_bytes, readBlock.tapeHdrSz(), n_read_bytes)`. Line 182: `decodeTapeBlockHdr(name_bytes, hdr_bytes, mLimitBlockNo)`. `name_bytes + hdr_bytes` is the design's `header_raw`.
  - Lines 191–218: header CRC. If `getWord(&hdr_CRC)` fails (line 195), the function returns false at line 198 (header CRC `unreadable`). A mismatch (line 203) sets `BLOCK_HDR_CRC_ERR` and does **not** abort, so data is still read after an invalid header CRC.
  - Lines 237–251: data. `mReader.readBytes(readBlock.data, block_len, n_read_bytes)` (line 239). `TapeReader::readBytes` (`shared/TapeReader.cpp` lines 6–18) appends each byte as it is read, so on failure `readBlock.data` holds the bytes read so far (the truncated case). The function then returns false at line 243.
  - Lines 259–280: data CRC. For BBC with `block_len > 0`, `getWord(&data_CRC)` (line 264). On failure it returns false at line 267 (data CRC `unreadable`, with the full payload already in `readBlock.data`). Upstream quirk: for a BBC block with `block_len == 0` the `else` branch (lines 271–280) still reads one byte with `readByte` and returns false at line 276 if that fails, although no CRC is checked. This matters for empty-payload handling.
  - Lines 283–291: a data CRC mismatch sets `BLOCK_DATA_CRC_ERR` and does **not** abort. The full payload and recorded CRC are both available. This is the damaged block 6 case (Requirement 2.3).
  - Lines 300–316: for the last BBC block (return false at line 308), the function requires a trailer tone of at least `minBlockTiming.trailerToneDuration` (0.5 s) and **returns false if none is found**, after the data CRC was read. A hook placed only on the success path would drop a fully read last block.
  - Natural hook points: one emit call before each `return false` after line 127 (with the partial state), and one after line 291 (CRC outcomes known), or a single emit in a wrapper around `readBlock` that uses `readBlock`, `readStatus`, and captured CRC values.
- `shared/FileDecoder.cpp` (384 lines): `bool FileDecoder::readFile(ostream& logFile, TapeFile& tapFile, string searchName, FileReadStatus &readStatus)` starts at line 43. It calls `mBlockDecoder.readBlock(...)` at line 112, computes `complete_block` at line 157, and zero-pads at lines 204–222 (`read_block.data.push_back(0x0)` at line 220). Observations must be taken before line 112 returns to this function, which means inside `BlockDecoder::readBlock`.
- Also noted for the fixture encoder (task 6.x): `shared/TapeProperties.h` lines 104–109 define `bbmTiming` with minimum timings (first-block prelude 3 cycles, first-block lead 2.0 s, other-block lead 0.2 s, trailer 0.5 s) and nominal timings (4 cycles, 5.1 s, 0.9 s, trailer 5.3 s, last-block gap 1.8 s). The first BBC block expects a dummy byte between the prelude and the lead tone (comment at lines 73–74 of `BlockDecoder.cpp`).
- `ScanTape/ArgParser.cpp`: options are parsed in a `strcmp` chain from line 94 (`-bbm` pre-scan at line 83, main-loop entry at line 129). Usage text is at lines 22–52. The new `-json` and `-capture-id` options go into this chain.

## Task 2.2: Write the JSON block-observation patch
Requirements: 1.2, 2.1, 2.2, 2.3, 2.4, 2.5, 2.6, 2.7
### Changes
`patches/atomtapeutils-json-observations.patch` (659 lines, LF only, SHA-256 `ba59709a2510553bddfbec07fd33603338af4de7f607d6606e165f829763c628`) is a `git diff` against a scratch copy of the vendored tree, whose tree id is `faf78c9b...`, the pinned revision. The vendored `third_party/AtomTapeUtils/` is unchanged. Files touched by the patch:
- `shared/ObservationWriter.h/.cpp` (new): a hand-written JSON emitter for `taperescue.observations/1`. Top level: `schema`, `capture_id`, `decoder {name, revision, mode: "bbm"}`, `blocks`. Each block carries `sample_offset`, `time_s`, `header_raw`, `header`, `header_crc`, `payload`, `expected_len`, `actual_len`, and `data_crc`. The document is written to `<path>.tmp` and then renamed into place.
- `shared/BlockDecoder.h/.cpp`: the upstream `readBlock` body is renamed `decodeBlock`. The new `readBlock` resets a `BlockObservation`, calls `decodeBlock`, and records the observation on every exit path once the `0x2a` sync byte has been read. The payload is copied from `readBlock.data`, which holds the bytes as read. This runs inside `BlockDecoder`, before `FileDecoder::readFile` zero-pads. Recording points:
  - the time at the sync byte's start bit
  - the name bytes and header bytes, also when the header was only partly read
  - `headerDecoded` and `expectedLen = readBlock.size`
  - the recorded header CRC and whether it matched
  - the recorded data CRC and whether it matched
- `shared/CMakeLists.txt`: adds `ObservationWriter.cpp` to the `shared` library and `ObservationWriter.h` to the installed headers.
- `ScanTape/ArgParser.h/.cpp`: adds the `-json <file>` and `-capture-id <hex>` options and their usage text. The parser rejects:
  - `-json` without `-bbm`
  - `-json` without `-capture-id`, or with a `-capture-id` that is not exactly 64 lowercase hex digits
  - `-capture-id` without `-json`
  - `-json` together with `-g`, `-uef`, `-wav`, `-csw`, `-tap`, `-ssd`, or `-c`
  - a missing option value

  These errors go to stderr with exit status 255.
- `ScanTape/ScanTape.cpp`: in `-json` mode:
  - UEF and CSW inputs are rejected, because observations need WAV sample positions.
  - No `tape.log` and no per-file outputs are written. The log goes to a null stream.
  - The JSON is written after the `readFile` loop. If the write fails, the decoder prints `Failed to write JSON observations: ...` and exits 255.
- `shared/WavCycleDecoder.cpp` (not in the task list; see the deviation below): `countHalfCycles` returns false when the samples end before a full bit duration.
### Field semantics (for task 5.1 and task 8)
- `sample_offset` is the sample index where the level decoder detects the start bit of the block's `0x2a` sync byte, and `time_s` is the same position in seconds (`%.6f`). On synthetic WAVs it was 1 sample after the computed start of the start bit, for all 9 blocks checked.
- `header_raw` covers the filename bytes, including the `0x00` terminator, through the next-file address. It excludes the sync byte and the header CRC. A partly read header keeps the bytes that were read.
- `header` holds the parsed fields only when the header CRC is `valid`. Otherwise it is `null`, which matches `models.py` ("None when the header CRC is not valid"). The fields are parsed from `header_raw`: `block_len` is the raw 16-bit length field and `flags` is the raw flag byte.
- `expected_len` is `readBlock.size`, the payload length the decoder tried to read. It is 0 when the header could not be fully read and decoded.
  - Edge case: upstream `updateFromBBMFlags` forces the size to 0 when flag bit 6 (empty block) is set. A header with bit 6 set and a non-zero length field would therefore give `expected_len = 0` with `header.block_len != 0`. The project Block_Builder does not produce such headers.
- `data_crc` is `null` when the header was decoded and `expected_len == 0`, because the BBC format has no data CRC for an empty block. Otherwise it is `{"outcome": "valid"|"invalid", "recorded": n}`, or `{"outcome": "unreadable"}` with no `recorded` key when the CRC bytes were not read. The same rule applies to `header_crc`.
- `actual_len` equals `len(payload)` and is never more than `expected_len`. A data or header CRC mismatch does not stop reading (upstream behaviour), so the full payload and the recorded CRC are both emitted.
- Blocks are written in sample-offset order. A block re-read after a `FileDecoder` rollback (a block from another file found while reading a file) has the same sample offset and replaces the earlier entry. The `rollback.wav` case below has no duplicates.
### Deviation: end-of-samples fix in `WavCycleDecoder::countHalfCycles`
The first build (before this fix) failed 2 of 95 checks. If a capture ended inside a byte frame, upstream `countHalfCycles` stopped at the end of the samples and still returned true. The missing bits then decoded as `1`, and a byte that was never recorded came back as read:
- A cut 2 to 7 bits into payload byte 101 gave `actual_len = 101`, with a last byte of `ff`, `fd`, `f5`, or `c5` against a recorded `85`.
- A cut inside the second data CRC byte gave `data_crc {"outcome": "invalid", "recorded": 0x53f3}` against a recorded `0x5393`, where the correct result is `unreadable`.

That violates Requirement 2.4 (emit only the bytes actually read) and Requirement 2.7 (an unreadable CRC is `unreadable`). The fix returns false when the bit window is cut off. The `getDataBit` caller already treats false as "unexpected end of samples". After the fix:
- A cut 0 to 9 bits into byte 101 gives `actual_len = 100`, with every byte equal to what was written. A byte whose stop bit is missing is dropped, which is the conservative choice.
- A cut 10 bits in (the full frame) gives 101, and a cut 20 bits in gives 102.

Effect on the stock (non-JSON) behaviour: on `trunc_crc.wav`, `-c` output now reports 1 complete block where stock reported 2, because stock had read the data CRC from missing samples. On the other 4 synthetic WAVs, the `-c` output was byte-identical to the stock decoder. On `multi.wav`, the `-g` output directory (`tape.log`, `.bbc`, `.dat`, `.inf`, `.tap`, `.uef`, bin) was identical to stock.

Observed per synthetic WAV (filenames `TEST`, `HDR`, `AAA`/`BBB`; payloads 256, 256, 100 bytes):
- `multi.wav` (3 blocks; block 1 has bit 2 of payload offset 17 flipped after its CRC was computed): 3 observations. In block 1, `data_crc {"outcome": "invalid", "recorded": 21395}` (the original CRC), `actual_len 256`, and the payload differs from the original only at offset 17. Blocks 0 and 2 are valid. Block 2 has `flags 128`. Header fields equal the written values.
- `trunc_payload.wav` (cut 100 payload bytes + 5 bits into block 1): block 1 has `expected_len 256`, `actual_len 100`, payload equal to the first 100 written bytes, no padding, and `data_crc {"outcome": "unreadable"}`. There is no observation after block 1.
- `trunc_crc.wav` (cut 5 bits into the second data CRC byte of block 1): block 1 has the complete 256-byte payload and `data_crc {"outcome": "unreadable"}`.
- `hdr_empty.wav` (block 1 header CRC XOR `0x0101`; block 2 empty with flags `0xC0`):
  - Block 1: `header null` and `header_crc {"outcome": "invalid", "recorded": <written value>}`. The data was still read (256 bytes, data CRC `valid`).
  - Block 2: `expected_len 0`, `actual_len 0`, `data_crc null`, and `header.flags 192`.
- `rollback.wav` (non-final block of `AAA` followed by file `BBB`): 3 observations with distinct sample offsets, so the `FileDecoder` rollback re-read produced no duplicates.
- `silent.wav` (1 s of silence): `{"schema": "taperescue.observations/1", "capture_id": "...", "decoder": {...}, "blocks": []}`, exit 0. The adapter must map zero blocks to `no_blocks` (Requirement 3.5), because the exit status does not signal it.
### Not covered here (task 8 and later)
- The checks used throwaway synthetic WAVs, not the project fixtures. Task 8 must repeat them on the STARMAP clean and damaged fixtures (8 intact blocks, block 6 `invalid` with the original CRC and differences at the recorded offsets, a WAV cut inside block 3, and a silent WAV).
- A header cut short (partial `header_raw`, `header_crc unreadable`, `expected_len 0`) and a header CRC that cannot be read were not exercised by a WAV. The code path records them, but no test has observed them.
- The final block is recorded even when its trailer tone is missing: the observation is emitted before `readBlock` returns false at the trailer check. No WAV exercised this; every synthetic last block had a 5.3 s trailer.
- Noise and dropouts inside a payload (as opposed to a cut at the end of the file) were not tested. If the reader resynchronises on noise, a "read" byte may not match the recording. Task 8 and the Requirement 2.9 check cover the damaged fixtures.
### Blockers
None.

## Task 3.1: Shared data models (`taperescue/models.py`)

Requirements: 2.2, 2.4, 2.6, 2.7

### Changes

- `taperescue/models.py`: literals `CrcOutcome`, `BlockStatus`, `UnresolvedReason` (plus helper `PayloadState`), and frozen dataclasses `HeaderFields`, `CrcReading`, `BlockObservation`, `CaptureObservations`, `RecoverOptions`, `BlockResult`, `RecoveryResult`, with field names and types as in the design's Data Models section.
  - `HeaderFields.identity_key()` returns `(filename, load_addr, exec_addr, block_no, block_len, flags)`. `next_addr` is excluded.
  - `CrcReading` rejects an unknown outcome, a value with `unreadable`, a missing value otherwise, and values outside `0..0xFFFF` (Requirement 2.7).
  - `BlockObservation` rejects `len(payload) != actual_len` and `actual_len > expected_len`, so a padded or overlong payload cannot be built (Requirements 2.4, 2.5).
  - `payload_state` is `empty` when `expected_len == 0` and the header CRC is valid, `truncated` when `actual_len < expected_len`, otherwise `complete`. A zero-byte read of a 256-byte block is `truncated`, not `empty` (Requirement 2.6).
  - `is_intact` requires a valid header CRC and either an empty payload or a complete payload with a valid data CRC.
  - `CaptureObservations` rejects observations whose `capture_id` differs from the capture's.
  - `RecoverOptions(reconstruct=True, candidate_cap=64)` lives in `models.py` as the task lists it. The engine can re-export it.
  - `BlockResult.reconstruction` is typed as `ReconstructionOutcome | None` through a `TYPE_CHECKING`-only import from `taperescue.engine.reconstruct`, so there is no runtime import of the engine. `BlockResult.is_recovered` is a small helper (status `Intact` or `Reconstructed · CRC match` with a payload).
  - `RecoveryResult.program_payload()` returns the concatenated payloads of blocks `0..final_block`, or `None` when `final_block` is `None`, a block is absent or duplicated, or any block is not recovered.
- `tests/test_models.py`: 13 unit tests covering the identity key, CRC reading invariants, complete, truncated, and empty payload states, intact checks with invalid headers, rejection of padding, capture ID consistency, immutability, option defaults, and `program_payload()`.

### Commands and results

| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_models.py -q` | `13 passed in 0.17s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `20 passed in 0.18s` (whole suite at the time of the run) |

None. `taperescue/engine/reconstruct.py` does not exist yet; the forward reference is only evaluated by type checkers.

## Task 3.2: Implement `taperescue/crc.py`
Requirements: 6.1, 6.2
### Changes
- `taperescue/crc.py`: table-driven `crc16_xmodem(data, init=0x0000) -> int`. Polynomial 0x1021 (`POLY`), MSB-first (no input or output reflection), final XOR 0x0000. The 256-entry table is built once at import. `init` outside `0..0xFFFF` raises `ValueError`. Passing a previous result as `init` continues the calculation.
### Commands and results
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `.venv/bin/python -c 'from taperescue.crc import crc16_xmodem as c; p=bytes((17*i+11*i*i+42)%256 for i in range(256)); v=b"123456789"; print(f"check={c(v):#06x} test_payload={c(p):#06x} len={len(p)}")'` | `check=0x31c3 test_payload=0xac93 len=256` |
| Ad hoc cross-check (inline `python -c`): 2000 random byte strings of length 0 to 299 (`random.Random(0)`), comparing against a bitwise reference loop and checking that appending the CRC high byte first gives residue 0 | `bitwise_mismatches=0 empty=0x0000` |

Both Requirement 6 vectors match: ASCII `123456789` gives `0x31C3` (6.1), and the Test_Payload `payload[i] = (17*i + 11*i*i + 42) mod 256` gives `0xAC93` (6.2). The cross-check was a one-off command line check. The formal vector tests belong to task 3.3 (`tests/test_crc.py`), which this task did not create.
### Blockers
None.

## Task 4.1: WAV validation (`taperescue/wavcheck.py`)
Requirements: 3.1, 3.2, 3.3
### Changes
- `taperescue/wavcheck.py`: `WavInfo`, `WavRejected(code, message)` with codes `not_riff_wave`, `unsupported_format`, `too_large`, `too_long`, `no_data`, constants `MAX_BYTES = 64 MiB` and `MAX_SECONDS = 600`, `inspect_wav_header(head, file_size)` and `validate_capture(path)`.
- The RIFF chunk list is walked directly, with odd-size padding handled and unknown chunks skipped. The file extension is not used. Accepted: format tag 1 (PCM), or `WAVE_FORMAT_EXTENSIBLE` (0xFFFE) whose sub-format GUID is the PCM GUID. The sample rate must be 44100 Hz, the samples 16-bit, the channel count 1, and the block align consistent with them.
- Check order: size (before any parsing), RIFF/WAVE magic and fmt chunk, format, data presence, then duration = `data_bytes / (sample_rate * block_align)`. Exactly 64 MiB and exactly 600 s are accepted.
- A data chunk size that extends past the end of the file is clamped to the bytes actually present.
- `validate_capture` opens the file read-only and seeks between chunk headers. It does not read the samples.
- Example rejection message: `Detected PCM, 48000 Hz, 16-bit, 2 channels; TapeRescue supports 44100 Hz, 16-bit, mono PCM`. The size and duration messages state the limit (`64 MiB (67108864 bytes)`, `10 minutes (600 s)`) and the detected value.
- `tests/test_wavcheck.py`: 26 unit tests that build WAVs in memory with `wave` and `struct`. They cover acceptance of plain PCM and of extensible PCM, chunk walking, header-only input, and the 600 s and 64 MiB boundaries. They also cover each rejection code: not RIFF/WAVE (5 inputs), unsupported format (rate, 8/24-bit, stereo, IEEE float, extensible with a non-PCM GUID or with no extension), too large (in memory, and as a sparse file on disk), too long, and no data (3 inputs).
### Commands and results

## Task 5.1: Implement `taperescue/decoder_adapter/schema.py`
Requirements: 2.2, 2.4, 2.5, 2.6, 2.7, 2.8
### Changes
- `taperescue/decoder_adapter/schema.py`:
  - `DecoderError(code, message, capture_id, detail=None)` with `code` one of `timeout`, `decoder_failed`, `invalid_output`, `no_blocks`. Attributes `code`, `message`, `capture_id`, `detail`. Task 5.2 (`run.py`) should import it from here.
  - `parse_observations(doc, expected_capture_id) -> CaptureObservations`. Every rule failure raises `DecoderError("invalid_output")` with the message `Decoder output was invalid for capture <id>: <rule detail>`. Any `ValueError` from the model constructors is converted the same way.
  - `observations_to_doc(capture) -> dict`, the inverse serialiser, so task 5.3 can test the Property 2 round trip.
  - `SCHEMA_ID = "taperescue.observations/1"`.
- `tests/decoder_adapter/test_schema.py`: 50 unit tests covering valid documents (failed complete block, intact block, truncated, empty, unreadable and invalid header CRC, block order, round trip) and each rejection rule.
### Schema rules enforced
- Top level: `schema` equals `taperescue.observations/1`; `capture_id` equals the expected ID; `blocks` is a list; `decoder` is optional and must be an object if present.
- Per block: `len(bytes.fromhex(payload)) == actual_len`; `actual_len <= expected_len`; `expected_len == header.block_len` when the header CRC is valid; a CRC object has `recorded` if and only if `outcome != "unreadable"`, with `recorded` an int in `0..0xFFFF`; a `valid` data CRC requires `actual_len == expected_len`.
- Payloads are kept at their actual length with no padding.
### Choices made where the design is ambiguous (for matching the C++ patch in task 8)
These follow the literal field names in the design's schema example.
1. **Unknown keys are rejected** at the top level, in blocks, in `header`, and in CRC objects. Allowed optional keys are `decoder` (top level) and `time_s` (block, a non-negative number, validated then discarded because the model has no field for it). The patch must not emit other keys.
2. **`data_crc` is `null` exactly when `expected_len == 0`.** Every block with `expected_len > 0` needs a `data_crc` object. A truncated block whose CRC bytes were never reached must emit `{"outcome": "unreadable"}`, not `null`.
3. **`header` with a non-valid header CRC.** A valid header CRC requires a `header` object. When the header CRC is `invalid` or `unreadable`, the patch may emit `null` or a parsed object. An object is type-checked and then dropped, because `BlockObservation.header` is `None` whenever the header CRC is not valid. `expected_len` is not compared with `block_len` in that case.
4. **Hex** must be pairs of `[0-9a-fA-F]` with no whitespace. Upper and lower case are both accepted, and the serialiser writes lowercase.
5. **Header field ranges**: `filename` is a string of at most 10 characters; `load_addr`, `exec_addr`, and `next_addr` are in `0..0xFFFFFFFF`; `block_no` and `block_len` are in `0..0xFFFF`; `flags` is in `0..0xFF`. Booleans are rejected wherever an integer is expected.
### Commands and results
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/decoder_adapter/test_schema.py -q` | `50 passed in 0.22s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `188 passed in 0.33s` |
### Blockers
None. Real decoder output has not been checked against this parser yet, because the patch (task 2.2) is still in progress. Task 8 should run `parse_observations` on the patched decoder's output for the STARMAP fixtures and fix whichever side differs.

## Task 5.2: Implement `taperescue/decoder_adapter/run.py`
Requirements: 1.5, 3.4, 3.5
### Changes
- `taperescue/decoder_adapter/run.py` (new):
  - `capture_id_for(path)`: lowercase SHA-256 hex of the file bytes, read in 1 MiB chunks.
  - `decoder_args(decoder, wav, out, capture_id) -> list[str]`: builds the argument list.
  - `decode_capture(wav, *, decoder, workdir, timeout_s=60.0) -> CaptureObservations`: resolves the paths, computes the capture ID, generates `out = workdir/observations-<uuid4 hex>.json`, and calls `subprocess.run(args, shell=False, timeout=timeout_s, capture_output=True, cwd=workdir, stdin=DEVNULL)`. After the decoder exits it reads `out`, parses it with `schema.parse_observations`, and removes `out` and `out.tmp` in a `finally` block.
  - `NO_BLOCKS_MESSAGE = "No BBC 1200-baud blocks were found in this capture"`.
  - `DecoderError` and `parse_observations` are imported from `schema.py`, not redefined.
- `taperescue/decoder_adapter/__init__.py`: re-exports `DecoderError`, `parse_observations`, `capture_id_for`, `decode_capture`, `decoder_args`, `NO_BLOCKS_MESSAGE`.
### Deviation: argument order
The task lists `[decoder, "-bbm", "-json", out, "-capture-id", cid, wav]`. Upstream `ScanTape/ArgParser.cpp` reads the input file from `argv[1]` (line 71) and starts option parsing at `argv[2]`, so that order fails: the decoder exits 255 with `WAV file '-bbm' cannot be opened!` on stdout and writes no JSON (checked below). `run.py` uses the order the patch accepts, the same one used in task 2.2:

```
[decoder, wav, "-bbm", "-json", out, "-capture-id", cid]
```

Because the WAV is `argv[1]` and is passed as an absolute path, a file name that starts with `-` cannot be read as an option.
### Error mapping
| Condition | `DecoderError.code` | `message` | `detail` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` (`subprocess.run` kills the child) | `timeout` | `Decoding timed out after 60 s` (uses `timeout_s`) | none |
| Decoder binary cannot be started (`OSError`) | `decoder_failed` | `Decoder failed on this capture` | OS error text |
| Non-zero exit | `decoder_failed` | `Decoder failed on this capture` | `exit status N: <stderr, or stdout if stderr is empty, truncated to 2 KB>`, which is also logged at WARNING |
| Output file missing or unreadable, or not JSON | `invalid_output` | `Decoder output was invalid for capture <id>: ...` | reason |
| Schema violation | `invalid_output` (raised by `parse_observations`) | as in task 5.1 | rule |
| No observation with `header_crc.outcome == "valid"` | `no_blocks` | `No BBC 1200-baud blocks were found in this capture` | none |

Notes:
- The stdout fallback is there because upstream ScanTape prints some input errors to stdout, for example `Input file has no data or is not a valid 8 or 16-bit PCM Wave file!`. The user-facing message stays generic, as in the design's error table.
- Schema validation runs before the `no_blocks` check, so a malformed document is reported as `invalid_output` even when it has no blocks.
- A document whose blocks all have invalid or unreadable header CRCs gives `no_blocks` (Requirement 3.5 says "no valid block header").
- `decode_capture` does not call `wavcheck.validate_capture`. Callers (CLI and API) validate first, following the design's pipeline.
### Commands and results
Throwaway script `/tmp/tr52_smoke.py` (not committed), run with `.venv/bin/python /tmp/tr52_smoke.py`. The silent WAV was generated with the `wave` module: 1 s of zero samples, 44100 Hz, 16-bit, mono.

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `capture_id_for(silent.wav)` vs `hashlib.sha256(bytes).hexdigest()` | Equal (`3fa20276…6615`) |
| `decode_capture(silent.wav, decoder=build/decoder/ScanTape, workdir=work)` | `DecoderError` `no_blocks`, `No BBC 1200-baud blocks were found in this capture`, and `capture_id` matches. `work/` is empty afterwards |
| Task-order argv run directly: `ScanTape -bbm -json work/x.json -capture-id <cid> silent.wav` | Exit 255. Stdout `WAV file '-bbm' cannot be opened!`. No `x.json` |
| `decode_capture` on a 21-byte non-WAV file | `decoder_failed`, detail `exit status 255: Input file has no data or is not a valid 8 or 16-bit PCM Wave file! …` |
| `decode_capture` with a fake decoder `#!/bin/sh\nsleep 30` and `timeout_s=1` | `timeout`, `Decoding timed out after 1 s`, elapsed 1.00 s |
| `decode_capture` with a decoder path that does not exist | `decoder_failed`, detail `[Errno 2] No such file or directory: …` |
| `decode_capture` on the task 2.2 synthetic WAVs in `/tmp/synth` | `multi.wav`: 3 observations (block 1 data CRC `invalid`, 256/256). `hdr_empty.wav`: 3 observations (block 1 header `invalid` with `header=None`; block 2 empty, `data_crc None`, 0/0). `trunc_payload.wav`: 2 observations (block 1 data CRC `unreadable`, 100/256). This real decoder output passes `parse_observations` with no schema mismatch |
| `work/` after all runs | Empty (`out` and `out.tmp` removed) |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `211 passed in 0.31s` |
### Not covered here
- No committed tests. `tests/decoder_adapter/test_run.py` belongs to optional task 5.4, which covers the list/`shell=False` check, the sleeping fake decoder, and the no-blocks fake decoder.
- On timeout, `subprocess.run` kills only the direct child process, not a process group. The real ScanTape starts no subprocesses, so this is enough for production. A fake decoder in task 5.4 that forks a grandchild holding the pipes could make `subprocess.run` wait for the grandchild. The `/bin/sh` fake above returned in 1.00 s, so in this run nothing waited on a grandchild.
- The STARMAP fixtures do not exist yet (task 6). Task 8 repeats these checks on them.
### Blockers
None.

## Task 6.1: BASIC tokenizer
Requirements: 4.2
### Changes
- `taperescue/fixtures/tokenizer.py`: `tokenize(source) -> bytes`, `detokenize(program) -> str`, `normalise(source) -> str`, `TokenizeError(ValueError)`, `KEYWORDS` (keyword, token, flag byte) in ROM table order, `TOKEN_TEXT` (token to keyword, including the statement-form pseudo-variables 0xCF to 0xD3).
- Line format `0x0D, line_hi, line_lo, length, body`, where the length byte counts the whole line including the 4-byte prefix (maximum 255). The program ends with `0x0D 0xFF`. Line numbers must be 0 to 32767 and strictly ascending.
- The tokenizer follows the BASIC II ROM `MATCH` routine:
  - Keywords match in ROM table order and the first match wins. The ROM puts every keyword before any shorter keyword that is its prefix (ENDPROC before END, GET$ before GET, INKEY$ before INKEY, MODE before MOD, ERROR before ERR), so the first match is always the longest match. A test checks this for every pair of keywords.
  - Text inside string literals is not tokenized, and neither is the rest of the line after `REM` or `DATA` (flag bit 5) or after a `*` command at the start of a statement.
  - The name after `PROC` or `FN` is not tokenized (flag bit 3). Hex constants after `&` are not tokenized.
  - If a word starts with something other than a keyword, the whole word is copied as text, so `XPRINT` stays a variable name.
  - A conditional keyword (flag bit 0) that is followed by a word character is treated as a variable name, for example `ENDX`. Keywords without that flag are tokenized at the start of a name, as the ROM does: `TOTAL` becomes `TO` + `TAL`.
  - `PTR`, `PAGE`, `TIME`, `LOMEM` and `HIMEM` get +0x40 at the start of a statement (flag bit 6). The start-of-statement state is set by `:`, `THEN`, `ELSE` and `ERROR` and cleared by flag bit 1, names, numbers and punctuation, as in the ROM.
- Rejections, all raised as `TokenizeError`:
  - A number in the line-number position after GOTO, GOSUB, THEN, ELSE, RESTORE, TRACE, AUTO, DELETE, LIST or RENUMBER (flag bit 4). These are the exact places where the ROM would emit `0x8D`. A computed `GOTO X` is not rejected, because the ROM stores it without `0x8D`.
  - Keyword abbreviations (`P.`).
  - Non-printable or non-ASCII source characters, and lines whose tokenized length is over 255 bytes.
  - In `detokenize`: `0x8D`, the unused token `0xCE`, a bad framing or length byte, and bytes after the terminator.
- Source format: `<line number> <body>`. One separator space after the line number is not stored, so indentation beyond it is kept. Blank lines are ignored. `detokenize(tokenize(src)) == normalise(src)`.
- `tests/test_tokenizer.py`: 49 unit tests covering token values, framing, longest match, untokenized regions, ROM quirks, rejections, and a STARMAP-style round trip.
### Token table sources
The full BASIC II table is included (121 keyword entries plus the five pseudo-variable statement tokens). A partial table would make the tokenizer disagree with the ROM on programs that use any omitted keyword. The table was checked against two sources:
1. The BBC BASIC II ROM keyword table (`TOKENS`, with flag bytes) and token constants in the annotated BASIC II/III disassembly at https://github.com/ivop/bbc-basic (`basic.s`). That disassembly is based on J.G. Harston's source reconstruction (https://mdfs.net/Software/BBCBasic/BBC/). A one-off script parsed the `dta` lines in the BASIC II branch and compared them with `KEYWORDS`: all 121 entries were identical in order, token value and flag byte.
2. The BBC BASIC program format page at https://fileformats.org/code/program/bbc/basic/, whose table was compared there against the BASIC II ROM image. A one-off script compared its token-to-keyword pairs with `TOKEN_TEXT`: 126 entries, no mismatches.

Every value named in the task matches both sources: PRINT &F1, MODE &EB, DEF &DD, PROC &F2, END &E0, FOR &E3, NEXT &ED, GCOL &E6, MOVE &EC, DRAW &DF, PLOT &F0, VDU &EF, READ &F3, DATA &DC, REM &F4, RESTORE &F7, RND &B3, ENDPROC &E1, TO &B8, STEP &88, IF &E7, THEN &8C, ELSE &8B, COLOUR &FB, CLS &DB, LOCAL &EA, SIN &B5, COS &9B, INT &A8, AND &80, OR &84, NOT &AC, REPEAT &F5, UNTIL &FD, GOTO &E5, GOSUB &E4. No token was left out for lack of verification.
### Commands and results
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `curl -sSL -o /tmp/bbcbasic.s https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ivop/bbc-basic/master/basic.s` | 15313 lines downloaded |
| `.venv/bin/python /tmp/check_tokens.py` (parses the ROM table and compares it with `KEYWORDS`) | `rom entries: 121 ours: 121`, `identical (order, token, flags): True` |
| `curl` of the fileformats.org page, then an inline comparison script against `TOKEN_TEXT` | `fileformats entries: 126 ours: 126`, `mismatches: {}` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_tokenizer.py -q` | `49 passed in 0.19s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `95 passed in 0.23s` |
### Blockers
None. Notes:
- How BBC BASIC stores the spaces between the line number and the body is not modelled. This tokenizer drops exactly one separator space. That affects program size by at most one byte per line and does not change how the program runs.
- The property test for the round trip (Property 4) belongs to task 6.2 and was not written here.

## Task 6.3: Block builder
Requirements: 4.3, 4.4, 4.5
### Changes
- `taperescue/fixtures/block_builder.py`:
  - `TapeBlock` (frozen): filename, load/exec/next addresses, block number, flags, payload, `header_raw`, `header_crc`, `data_crc` (None for an empty block), and a `block_len` property.
  - `ByteFrame` (frozen): `value`, `role` (`sync`, `header`, `header_crc`, `payload`, `data_crc`), and `offset` within that role. For payload frames `offset` is the payload offset, which is what the damage injector (task 6.6) targets. CRC frames use offset 0 for the high byte and 1 for the low byte.
  - `encode_header(...)`: filename (1-10 printable ASCII characters, no spaces) + `0x00`, then load (4 LE), exec (4 LE), block number (2 LE), block length (2 LE), flags (1), next-file address (4 LE). Rejects out-of-range fields.
  - `build_blocks(program, filename="STARMAP", load_addr=0xFFFF0E00, exec_addr=0xFFFF8023, next_addr=0)`: 256-byte blocks except the last, numbered from 0. Flag bit 7 on the last block only. Bit 6 only for an empty block (an empty program gives one block with flags `0xC0` and no data CRC). A program that is an exact multiple of 256 bytes gets no extra empty block. Header CRC over `header_raw` and data CRC over the payload, both from `crc16_xmodem`.
  - `block_frames(block)`: tape order sync `0x2A`, header bytes, header CRC high byte first, payload, data CRC high byte first. Payload and data CRC are omitted for empty blocks.
  - Imports only `taperescue.crc`, not `taperescue.decoder_adapter`.
- `tests/test_block_builder.py`: 15 unit tests. They cover block count and sizes for a 1800-byte program (8 blocks), the final flag, exact multiples of 256, the header layout, CRC values, frame roles and offsets and CRC byte order, the empty-program block, rejection of bad filenames and out-of-range fields, and the import check. One test writes the frames of all 8 blocks into a hand-built UEF (`0x0100` chunks) and parses it with the independent `taperescue.uef.reader`. The reader's header fields, payloads, and stored CRCs equal the builder's, and the reader's own bitwise CRC confirms both CRCs.

## Task 6.5: FSK modulator
Requirements: 4.7, 4.8, 4.9
### Changes
- `taperescue/fixtures/fsk.py` (new):
  - Segments: `Carrier(cycles)` is continuous 2400 Hz. The count must be even because a `1` bit cell holds two cycles. `Gap(cells)` is silence measured in bit cells. `ByteFrames(values)` takes ints or `block_builder.ByteFrame` objects and writes each byte as a start bit `0`, eight data bits LSB first, and a stop bit `1`.
  - A `0` cell is one cycle of 1200 Hz and a `1` cell is two cycles of 2400 Hz. Cell `k`, counted from the start of the stream, covers samples `floor(k*147/4)` to `floor((k+1)*147/4)` (`bit_start_sample`), so every cell is 36 or 37 samples. The arithmetic is integer only.
  - `SINE_TABLE`: 256 int16 values at amplitude 16000, written out as a literal so the output does not depend on libm. Sample `i` of a cell with `n` samples and `c` cycles is `SINE_TABLE[(256*c*i)//n % 256]`. Each cell starts at phase 0, so tone changes happen at a zero crossing. The six possible cell waveforms are precomputed.
  - `wav_header`/`modulate`: a hand-written 44-byte RIFF header (PCM, 44100 Hz, 16-bit, mono, no other chunks), followed by little-endian samples.
  - `TapeTiming` and `BBC_TIMING`: the single table of lead-in constants, in integer cycles or cells. The comment table in the module cites the source for each value:

    | Field | Value | Source |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | `leading_gap_bits` | 600 cells (0.5 s) | Room before the first tone |
    | `first_prelude_cycles` | 4 | AtomTapeUtils `bbmTiming` nominal prelude (minimum 3) |
    | `dummy_byte` | `0xAA` | BBC dummy byte between prelude and lead tone (`BlockDecoder.cpp` comment) |
    | `first_lead_cycles` | 12240 (5.1 s) | BBC: 5.1 s of 2400 Hz leader before a file ([DSP Stack Exchange](https://dsp.stackexchange.com/questions/72332/convert-old-computer-cassette-signal-into-binary)). Decoder minimum 2.0 s |
    | `other_lead_cycles` | 2160 (0.9 s) | BBC: 0.9 s between blocks recorded in one go (same source). Decoder minimum 0.2 s |
    | `trailer_cycles` | 4800 (2.0 s) | Design value. Decoder needs at least 0.5 s after the last block (nominal 5.3 s) |
    | `trailing_gap_bits` | 1200 cells (1.0 s) | Silence at the end |
  - `tape_stream(frame_lists, timing)` builds the segments for one file: leading gap, then prelude, dummy byte, and 5.1 s lead before block 0, and 0.9 s lead before every later block. The block frames follow each lead, and the file ends with the trailer and the trailing gap.
  - `segment_start_samples(stream)` returns the start sample of every segment plus the total sample count. Tests use it to compare against the decoder's `sample_offset`.
  - The module imports only `struct`, `dataclasses`, and `typing`. It imports nothing from `taperescue`, so nothing from `taperescue.decoder_adapter`, and it never runs the decoder.
- `tests/test_fsk.py` (new, 18 tests):
  - Bit boundaries equal `floor(k*147/4)` for k < 10000, and 1200 cells are exactly 44100 samples.
  - The sine table matches its definition.
  - `wavcheck.inspect_wav_header` reads the header as PCM 44100/16/1.
  - A `0` cell has one cycle, a `1` cell has two cycles, and every cell starts at 0.
  - Byte frame bit order is correct for `0b11001010`.
  - Carrier and gap lengths are correct.
  - `ByteFrames` accepts `block_builder` frames.
  - Odd or negative carriers, negative gaps, out-of-range bytes, and an empty block list are rejected.
  - Timing values are checked against the BBC and decoder minimums.
  - The `tape_stream` layout is checked.
  - Output is byte-identical across two runs.
  - An AST check confirms there are no `taperescue`, `subprocess`, or `os` imports and no `ScanTape` reference.
  - Integration test (skipped with a reason when `build/decoder/ScanTape` is missing): runs the real decoder on an 8-block program (see below).
### Real decoder check
Program: `random.Random(65).randbytes(1800)` (non-periodic, so every block has a different data CRC), built with `build_blocks` (8 blocks: 7 × 256 bytes and 1 × 8 bytes) and modulated with `tape_stream` + `modulate` using `BBC_TIMING`. The WAV is 2,814,506 bytes (31.91 s), SHA-256 `9f8b89e54f0edeefaf548288715dce9ec5d9e9d54feb5b7a1fb90dd77c4c98ef`.

Command (throwaway `/tmp/fsk_check.py`, which writes `/tmp/fsk65/dummy.wav`):
```
build/decoder/ScanTape /tmp/fsk65/dummy.wav -bbm -json /tmp/fsk65/dummy.json -capture-id 9f8b89e54f0edeefaf548288715dce9ec5d9e9d54feb5b7a1fb90dd77c4c98ef
```
Exit 0, empty stdout and stderr, and 8 observations:

| block | header CRC | data CRC | len | payload = input | header_raw = input | flags | sample_offset | sync start (computed) | time_s |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | valid 31169 | valid 44157 | 256/256 | yes | yes | 0 | 247402 | 247401 | 5.610023 |
| 1 | valid 37602 | valid 50906 | 256/256 | yes | yes | 0 | 392197 | 392196 | 8.893356 |
| 2 | valid 49062 | valid 21929 | 256/256 | yes | yes | 0 | 536992 | 536991 | 12.176689 |
| 3 | valid 21637 | valid 54332 | 256/256 | yes | yes | 0 | 681787 | 681786 | 15.460023 |
| 4 | valid 58670 | valid 58317 | 256/256 | yes | yes | 0 | 826582 | 826581 | 18.743356 |
| 5 | valid 3597 | valid 55219 | 256/256 | yes | yes | 0 | 971377 | 971376 | 22.026689 |
| 6 | valid 9033 | valid 40557 | 256/256 | yes | yes | 0 | 1116172 | 1116171 | 25.310023 |
| 7 | valid 15543 | valid 53304 | 8/8 | yes | yes | 128 | 1260967 | 1260966 | 28.593356 |

Each recorded CRC equals the CRC `block_builder` wrote, and the concatenated payloads equal the 1800-byte program. `sample_offset` is 1 sample after the computed start of the sync byte's start bit, the same offset that task 2.2 found. The first modulator version decoded correctly, so no timing changes were needed.

Timing margin (throwaway `/tmp/fsk_margin.py`, same program, one field changed per run). Every run gave exit 0, 8 blocks, and 8 intact blocks equal to the input:
- `first_prelude_cycles` 2 and 0
- the decoder minimums: lead 2.0 s, inter-block 0.2 s, trailer 0.5 s
- first lead 1.8 s, which is below the decoder's 2.0 s minimum
- no leading gap

The decoder therefore accepts a wide range around these values. The fixtures use the BBC values above.
### Commands and results
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_fsk.py -v` | First run: 17 passed, 1 failed. A slice in the test compared `wav[8:16]` with `b"WAVE"`; the test was fixed to `b"WAVEfmt "`, and the modulator was not changed. Second run: `18 passed in 0.28s`, with the integration test run, not skipped |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `229 passed in 0.48s` |
| `.venv/bin/python /tmp/fsk_check.py` | See the table above. Modulating and decoding together take about 0.13 s |
### Notes
- Carrier lengths must be even cycle counts so every segment fills whole bit cells and boundaries stay on the global `floor(k*147/4)` grid.
- For the UEF exporter task, not changed here: the design gives the UEF `0x0110` carrier as "5100 cycles before block 0, 1800 before other blocks". If chunk `0x0110` counts cycles at twice the base frequency (2400 Hz), as the UEF format describes, then 5.1 s is 12240 cycles and 0.9 s is 2160 cycles, the same values as `BBC_TIMING`. This should be checked when the exporter is written.
- No property test was added. Property 6 (deterministic fixture generation) belongs to task 6.9. A plain determinism unit test is included.
### Blockers
None.

## Task 6.6: Damage injector
Requirements: 5.1, 5.3, 5.4
### Changes
- `taperescue/fixtures/damage.py`:
  - `BitFlip(block_no, offset, bit)` (frozen): `offset` is the payload offset, `bit` is one bit index 0-7 (0 is least significant). `__post_init__` rejects non-integer fields (including `bool`), a block number outside 16 bits, a negative offset, and a bit index outside 0-7, so a multi-bit mask such as `0x30` cannot be passed as a bit. `mask` returns `1 << bit`.
  - `apply_damage(frames, block_no, flips) -> list[ByteFrame]`: returns a new frame list for block `block_no` with each flip XORed into the payload frame at that payload offset. It rejects a flip that names another block, a flip whose offset is not an existing payload frame (offsets past the payload, which is where the data CRC frames sit, and any flip on an empty block), and a repeated offset, so each damaged byte differs from the original by exactly one bit. The sync, header, header CRC, and data CRC frames are copied unchanged and no CRC is recomputed. Frame count, order, roles, and offsets are unchanged, so the modulator produces the same start bits, stop bits, and timing. The input list is not modified.
  - Imports only `taperescue.fixtures.block_builder`, not `taperescue.decoder_adapter`.
- `tests/test_damage.py`: 23 unit tests on block 6 of an 1800-byte program using the planned A flips (offset 17 bit 2, offset 143 bit 5). They check that only the two payload frames change, each by one bit; that roles, offsets, and order are unchanged; that the sync, header, header CRC, and data CRC frames are unchanged, the data CRC frames still hold the original CRC, and the damaged payload fails it; that the input is not mutated; a flip on the short last block; and rejection of offsets 256, 257, and 300, flips on an empty block, repeated offsets, a flip for another block, bit indexes -1, 8, `0x30`, and 255, non-integer fields, and a negative offset. One test checks the import graph.
### Commands and results
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_damage.py -q` | `23 passed in 0.19s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `211 passed in 0.37s` |
### Blockers
None. Notes:
- The check that A and B offsets are disjoint (Requirement 5.2) needs both damage plans, so it belongs to the fixture generator (task 6.8).
- Checking the modulated start bits, stop bits, and sample count needs the FSK modulator (task 6.5, running in parallel). Property 7 (task 6.7) covers that.

## Task 6.8: STARMAP and the fixture generator
Requirements: 4.1, 4.3, 4.6, 4.10, 5.2, 5.5
### Changes
- `fixtures/starmap/STARMAP.bas` (new): original BBC BASIC II program, 106 lines. It sets `MODE 1`, turns the cursor off, sets the palette (logical 1 blue, 2 cyan, 3 white), and seeds the generator with `seed=RND(-42)`. It then draws a 360-point Milky Way band, 240 stars in three brightness classes (the brightest drawn as crosses), a crescent moon made of two filled discs, a meteor, filled hills along the horizon, four constellation figures (Plough, Cassiopeia, Orion, Cygnus) read from `DATA` by `PROCconstellations`, W/N/E compass labels, and a title with a star count, then `END`. It uses only structured code: PROC, FOR/NEXT, IF/THEN/ELSE, and a plain `RESTORE`. It has no line-number references.
- `taperescue/fixtures/starmap.py` (new):
  - `build_program(source)` tokenizes the source and finds the `DEF PROCconstellations` line and its `ENDPROC` with `program_lines()`, which gives the offset and detokenized text of each line. It checks that the main program calls `PROCconstellations` before its `END` line.
  - If the DEF line starts below offset 1536, REM lines are inserted just before it. If the program is under 1793 bytes, REM lines are added at the end. Each REM line is 5 to 255 bytes. The function raises `StarmapLayoutError` in these cases: the procedure is not inside offsets 1536–1791, the length is outside 1793–2048, the block count is not 8, or there are no free line numbers for padding. The checked-in source needs no padding (`padding_lines: []`).
  - `select_damage(payload, crc)` tries the planned flips first (A: 17 bit 2, 143 bit 5; B: 64 bit 0, 201 bit 6), then `fallback_plans()`: 64 plans from `random.Random(0x5747)`, each with four distinct offsets. `check_plan` rejects a plan if the A and B offsets overlap, an offset repeats, a damaged payload still matches the original CRC, or the number of the 16 original/damaged combinations matching the CRC is not exactly 1. Rejected plans and the chosen source are written to `damage.json`.
  - `generate(out_dir, source=None, timing=BBC_TIMING)` writes `clean.wav`, `damaged_a.wav` and `damaged_b.wav` (`block_frames` → `apply_damage` on block 6 → `tape_stream` → `modulate`), `reference_payloads.json` and `damage.json`. JSON is written with `sort_keys=True`, `indent=2`, and no timestamps or paths. The function copies the source into `out_dir` when `out_dir` is not the source directory, and returns `GeneratedFixtures` with the WAV SHA-256 values. Task 7's `taperescue fixtures` command can call it directly.
  - The module imports nothing from `taperescue.decoder_adapter`, does not use `subprocess`, and has no `ScanTape` reference.
- `reference_payloads.json`: filename, load and exec addresses, program length and SHA-256, the PROCconstellations offset range, padding lines, WAV SHA-256 values, and for each block: `block_no`, `block_len`, `flags`, `next_addr`, `header_raw`, `header_crc`, `data_crc`, `hex`, `sha256`.
- `damage.json`: block 6 data CRC, flips for A and B (offset, bit, mask, original and damaged values), the CRCs of the damaged payloads, the plan source with the fallback seed and rejected plans, the candidate check, and the damaged WAV SHA-256 values.
- `tests/test_starmap_generate.py` (new, 11 tests): listing round trip; 8 blocks named STARMAP with flag 0x80 on block 7 only; PROCconstellations inside 1536–1791 and called before END; padding of a shortened source; rejection of an over-long source; planned plan accepted and overlapping plan rejected; fixed fallback list; consistency of the JSON records and WAV headers; determinism across two runs; checked-in fixtures equal to fresh generator output; generator import check.
### Layout of the generated program
| Item | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Tokenized length | 2025 bytes (7 × 256 + 233) |
| `DEF PROCconstellations` through `ENDPROC` | offsets 1538–1771 (block 6 is 1536–1791) |
| Main program call | line 150 `PROCconstellations`, before line 190 `END` |
| Constellation `DATA` | line 4000 at 1772–1778, line 4010 (Plough) at 1779–1848, crossing into block 7; lines 4020–4040 at 1849–2022; terminator at 2023–2024 |
| Block 7 | 233 bytes (offsets 1792–2024), all constellation `DATA` plus the terminator, flags 0x80 |
| Padding lines | none |
| Listing round trip | `detokenize(program) == normalise(source)`: True, 106 lines |

Block data CRCs: 0: 27331, 1: 20048, 2: 4934, 3: 39506, 4: 9147, 5: 43698, 6: 35417, 7: 59102. Program SHA-256 `4ac213a0e014229eb195bd7380962b9f4528147838ec9f1f7cb00be165e06e3a`.

Block 6 damage: the planned plan was accepted, so no fallback was used.

| Fixture | Offset | Bit | Original | Damaged | Damaged payload CRC |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A | 17 | 2 | 97 (`a`) | 101 (`e`) | 50936 |
| A | 143 | 5 | 52 (`4`) | 20 (DC4) | |
| B | 64 | 0 | 224 | 225 | 26293 |
| B | 201 | 6 | 105 (`i`) | 41 (`)`) | |

Candidate check: 16 candidates, 1 match (the original payload, CRC 35417).

WAV files (2,979,880 bytes each, 44100 Hz, 16-bit, mono):

| File | SHA-256 |
| --- | --- |
| clean.wav | `77fef8d5333430eb2afe524158a53f40a8d4e7c7897cf1c2337e6066f6e7d117` |
| damaged_a.wav | `2e09d3ce8d3bd4b042958c15dfb9d2d5a08774cf28ecbcff49e3bf06339d4c0b` |
| damaged_b.wav | `83a0ab0428d8299299f810f8ceb1df25c5781b7f8b8e29b34cb34794c735d0a0` |

Running `generate('fixtures/starmap')` a second time produced the same SHA-256 for all six files.
### Real decoder check
Throwaway script `/tmp/starmap_check.py`. For each WAV it runs the patched decoder with an argument list, parses the output with `decoder_adapter.schema.parse_observations`, and compares the result with `reference_payloads.json` and `damage.json`. Commands:
```
build/decoder/ScanTape fixtures/starmap/clean.wav -bbm -json /tmp/starmap_clean.wav.json -capture-id 77fef8d5333430eb2afe524158a53f40a8d4e7c7897cf1c2337e6066f6e7d117
build/decoder/ScanTape fixtures/starmap/damaged_a.wav -bbm -json /tmp/starmap_damaged_a.wav.json -capture-id 2e09d3ce8d3bd4b042958c15dfb9d2d5a08774cf28ecbcff49e3bf06339d4c0b
build/decoder/ScanTape fixtures/starmap/damaged_b.wav -bbm -json /tmp/starmap_damaged_b.wav.json -capture-id 83a0ab0428d8299299f810f8ceb1df25c5781b7f8b8e29b34cb34794c735d0a0
```
All three exit 0 with empty stderr. Each damaged run prints the upstream message `At least one block is corrupted (incorrect CRC) for file 'STARMAP' [00:00:00.5081 (0.5081s),00:00:32.7856 (32.79s)]` to stdout.

clean.wav: 8 observations, all header CRCs and data CRCs `valid`. Every `header_raw` and recorded CRC equals the reference, every payload equals the reference, and block 7 has flags 0x80. The concatenated payload is 2025 bytes with SHA-256 `4ac213a0…6e3a`, equal to `program_sha256`.

| block | header CRC | data CRC | recorded data CRC | len | flags | sample_offset |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | valid 31169 | valid | 27331 | 256/256 | 0x00 | 247402 |
| 1 | valid 37602 | valid | 20048 | 256/256 | 0x00 | 392197 |
| 2 | valid 49062 | valid | 4934 | 256/256 | 0x00 | 536992 |
| 3 | valid 21637 | valid | 39506 | 256/256 | 0x00 | 681787 |
| 4 | valid 58670 | valid | 9147 | 256/256 | 0x00 | 826582 |
| 5 | valid 3597 | valid | 43698 | 256/256 | 0x00 | 971377 |
| 6 | valid 9033 | valid | 35417 | 256/256 | 0x00 | 1116172 |
| 7 | valid 39989 | valid | 59102 | 233/233 | 0x80 | 1260967 |

damaged_a.wav and damaged_b.wav: blocks 0–5 and 7 match the clean table exactly (intact, payload equal to the reference). Block 6:

| Fixture | header CRC | data CRC | recorded data CRC | len | payload differs from reference at | values at those offsets |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A | valid 9033 | invalid | 35417 (original) | 256/256 | [17, 143] | 101, 20 (the damaged values) |
| B | valid 9033 | invalid | 35417 (original) | 256/256 | [64, 201] | 225, 41 (the damaged values) |

The script printed `ALL CHECKS PASSED`.
### Commands and results
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `.venv/bin/python -c "from taperescue.fixtures.starmap import generate; generate('fixtures/starmap')"` | Six files written; program 2025 bytes, proc 1538–1771, no padding, planned damage plan |
| `.venv/bin/python /tmp/starmap_check.py` | `ALL CHECKS PASSED` (tables above) |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_starmap_generate.py -q` | `11 passed in 0.38s` (first run) |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `240 passed in 0.68s` |
### Notes
- The first draft of STARMAP was 2299 bytes, with the DEF line at offset 1730. Header and per-procedure comment lines were removed, and the two comment lines describing the DATA format were replaced by one line, `2900 REM Constellations`, in front of the procedure. The final source lays out without padding. The padding code path is still tested with a shortened source; it inserts one 131-byte REM line, number 2901, and moves the DEF line to offset 1536.
- Block 6 is required to run the program to completion: it holds the whole of `PROCconstellations`, which line 150 calls before `END`. If the procedure were missing, BBC BASIC would stop with `No such PROC`.
- The formal integration test for the decoder output (task 8) and the property test for determinism (task 6.9) were not written here.
### Blockers
None. Pending, not claimed: running STARMAP from `clean.wav` or a UEF in JSBeeb. The program has only been checked as tokenized bytes and through the listing round trip, not executed on an emulated BBC Micro.

## Task 7: First CLI commands (`fixtures`, `decode`)
Requirements: 16.1, 16.3
### Changes
- `taperescue/cli.py`: argparse CLI with `--version` and two subcommands.
  - `taperescue fixtures [--out DIR]` calls `taperescue.fixtures.starmap.generate(DIR)` (default `fixtures/starmap`, relative to the working directory) and prints the output directory, program size, damage plan source, and WAV SHA-256 values.
  - `taperescue decode CAPTURE.wav [--decoder PATH] [-o OUT.json] [--timeout S]` runs `wavcheck.validate_capture` first, then checks that the decoder exists and is executable, then calls `decode_capture` with a `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory` as `workdir`. The decoder is `--decoder`, else `$TAPERESCUE_DECODER`, else `build/decoder/ScanTape` in the repository. The observations are serialised with `schema.observations_to_doc` as `json.dumps(sort_keys=True, indent=2)` to stdout, or to `OUT.json` with a one-line confirmation on stdout. `--timeout` defaults to the adapter's 60 s; it was added so the timeout path can be tested in under a second. `-o` pointing at the input capture is refused.
  - Every failure prints `error: <reason>` as the last stderr line and returns a non-zero status. argparse usage errors use the same `error:` prefix (custom `ArgumentParser.error`).
- `tests/test_cli.py` (new, 17 tests): the failure paths below, the `python -m taperescue.cli` entry point, `fixtures --out tmp` byte-equal to the checked-in fixtures, and three decode tests that are skipped when `build/decoder/ScanTape` is missing. Fake decoders for the non-zero exit, invalid output, and timeout cases are small shell scripts written to `tmp_path`.

### Exit codes
| Code | Meaning | Source in the design's Error Handling table |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Other failure (fixture layout error, output cannot be written) | |
| 2 | Usage error (no command, unknown command, `-o` equals the input) | |
| 3 | Capture missing, unreadable, or rejected by wavcheck | wavcheck rows |
| 4 | Export refused (reserved; the design fixes CLI exit 4 for `ExportRefused`) | Exporter, gate refuses |
| 5 | Decoder timeout | Adapter, decoder exceeds 60 s |
| 6 | Decoder missing, cannot start, or non-zero exit | Adapter, non-zero exit |
| 7 | Decoder output invalid | Adapter, schema violation |
| 8 | No BBC 1200-baud blocks | Adapter, no valid header |

### Commands and results
`.venv/bin/taperescue fixtures` exited 0 and printed:
```
wrote STARMAP fixtures to fixtures/starmap
program: 2025 bytes in 8 blocks
damage plan: planned
77fef8d5333430eb2afe524158a53f40a8d4e7c7897cf1c2337e6066f6e7d117  clean.wav
2e09d3ce8d3bd4b042958c15dfb9d2d5a08774cf28ecbcff49e3bf06339d4c0b  damaged_a.wav
83a0ab0428d8299299f810f8ceb1df25c5781b7f8b8e29b34cb34794c735d0a0  damaged_b.wav
```
These equal `wav_sha256` in `reference_payloads.json` and `damage.json`. `sha256sum fixtures/starmap/*` taken before and after the run (all six files, including both JSON files and `STARMAP.bas`) was identical. The fixture files are not yet committed, so the comparison is against the on-disk files from task 6.8 rather than `git diff`.

`.venv/bin/taperescue decode fixtures/starmap/clean.wav` exited 0: capture `77fef8d5…d117`, 8 observations, blocks 0–7, every header CRC and data CRC `valid`.

| block | header CRC | data CRC (recorded) | len | flags |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | valid 31169 | valid 27331 | 256/256 | 0x00 |
| 1 | valid 37602 | valid 20048 | 256/256 | 0x00 |
| 2 | valid 49062 | valid 4934 | 256/256 | 0x00 |
| 3 | valid 21637 | valid 39506 | 256/256 | 0x00 |
| 4 | valid 58670 | valid 9147 | 256/256 | 0x00 |
| 5 | valid 3597 | valid 43698 | 256/256 | 0x00 |
| 6 | valid 9033 | valid 35417 | 256/256 | 0x00 |

`decode fixtures/starmap/damaged_a.wav` and `decode fixtures/starmap/damaged_b.wav` both exited 0 with 8 observations. Blocks 0–5 and 7 equal the clean table. Block 6 in each: header CRC `valid 9033`, data CRC `invalid` with recorded value 35417 (the original CRC), 256/256 bytes.

### Notes
- When the decoder exits non-zero, the adapter's `log.warning` with the decoder output reaches stderr through Python's last-resort logging handler, before the `error:` line. The CLI does not configure logging.

## Task 8: Check decoder observations on the generated fixtures
Requirements: 2.1, 2.3, 2.4, 2.9, 3.5, 4.11, 5.6, 5.7
### Changes
- `tests/integration/test_fixture_decode.py` (new, 5 tests). Every test runs the real patched decoder through `taperescue.decoder_adapter.decode_capture`, which uses a real subprocess on real WAVs. Results are compared with `fixtures/starmap/reference_payloads.json` and `damage.json`. A module-level `skipif` skips the tests when `build/decoder/ScanTape` is missing or not executable, with the reason `patched decoder not built at <path>; run scripts/build_decoder.sh to enable the fixture decode integration tests`.
  - `test_clean_fixture_decodes_to_reference`: one observation for each of blocks 0–7. Every block is intact: header CRC valid, data CRC valid, and the recorded header CRC, data CRC, `header_raw`, filename, load and exec addresses, block length, flags, and payload all equal the reference. Only block 7 has flag bit 7. The concatenated length is 2025.
  - `test_damaged_fixture_block6_raw_observation[a|b]`: blocks 0–5 and 7 are intact and equal the reference. Block 6 has a valid header, `payload_state == "complete"`, 256/256 bytes, and data CRC `invalid` with `recorded` equal to the original CRC (reference and `damage.json`). The payload XOR the reference payload is exactly the `damage.json` `{offset: mask}` map for that side, and the observed values equal the `damaged` values.
  - `test_wav_cut_inside_block3_gives_truncated_observation`: the test builds `cut_block3.wav` in `tmp_path` from the 44-byte header and sample data of `clean.wav`, with the RIFF and `data` sizes rewritten by `fsk.wav_header`. The cut sample is block 3's clean `sample_offset` plus `ceil(((1 + 25 + 2 + 100) * 10 + 5) * 147 / 4)` samples. That is 100 whole payload bytes after the sync byte, the 25-byte `header_raw`, and the header CRC, plus 5 bits into payload byte 101. Assertions:
    - Blocks 0–3 are present and no block follows.
    - Blocks 0–2 are intact and equal the reference.
    - Block 3 has a valid header, `expected_len 256`, `actual_len 100`, and `payload_state "truncated"`.
    - Block 3's payload equals the first 100 reference bytes, with no padding.
    - Block 3's data CRC is `unreadable` with no recorded value.
  - `test_silent_wav_gives_no_blocks`: 1 s of zero samples (44.1 kHz, 16-bit, mono) raises `DecoderError` with code `no_blocks` and the message `No BBC 1200-baud blocks were found in this capture`.
- No change to the patch, the adapter, or any other file. The hook did not need to move to the byte reader (Requirement 2.9).

## Task 9.1: Implement the engine skeleton with intact selection
Requirements: 3.6, 8.1, 8.2, 8.3, 8.4, 9.11
### Changes
- `taperescue/engine/__init__.py`: `recover(captures, options=RecoverOptions())` as a pure function over `CaptureObservations`. `RecoverOptions` is imported from `taperescue.models` and re-exported, not redefined. Steps:
  - Normalise: group by `capture_id`, keep one copy per ID (the copy with the smallest canonical `repr`, so the choice never depends on input order), record each dropped copy in `duplicates_merged`, sort captures by ID and each capture's reads by `(block_no, sample_offset)`.
  - Place reads at block numbers. Valid-header reads use their parsed `block_no`. Invalid or unreadable-header reads supply no identity but are listed under a block for the report: the block number in their raw header bytes if it lies between the neighbouring valid block numbers in the same capture (inclusive), or at most one past the last valid block; otherwise the single gap between neighbours that are two apart; otherwise unplaced (kept in the capture, in no block).
  - Final block: lowest `block_no` among valid-header reads with flag bit 7. Required blocks `0..final`, or `0..max(placed block_no)` when no block is flagged (`final_block = None`; the gate in task 9.2 refuses export).
  - `DECISION_STEPS` tuple applied in order; first non-`None` result wins, else `selection.undecided`.
- `taperescue/engine/selection.py`: `BlockContext` (block number, reads sorted by `(capture_id, sample_offset)`, capture count, options; `valid_reads`, `intact_reads`), and steps:
  - a `step_a_missing`: no reads → `Missing`, `block_missing`.
  - b `step_b_header_invalid`: no valid-header read → `Unresolved`, `header_invalid`.
  - e `step_e_intact`: intact reads with one shared payload → `Intact`, payload (including `b""` for empty blocks), supporting IDs = every capture with an intact read, sorted. Declines when intact payloads differ (left to step d).
  - f `step_f_single_capture`: one capture and no intact read → `CRC failed`, `single_read`.
  - `undecided` fallback: `CRC failed`, reason `None`, no payload, no reconstruction (same shape as design step g).
- `tests/engine/test_selection.py`: 15 byte-array tests (no WAVs).

### Extension points and interim behaviour
- Task 11.1 inserts steps c (header disagreement) and d (payload conflict) between `step_b_header_invalid` and `step_e_intact` in `DECISION_STEPS`, and fills `anchors` (currently always `()`). Until then two captures with no anchor are not rejected, and intact reads with different payloads fall through to `undecided`.
- Task 12.2 appends steps g to l after `step_f_single_capture`. Until then a block that fails in every read across two or more captures reports `CRC failed` with reason `None` via `undecided`. It never carries a payload, so it cannot be exported.

### Commands and results
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/engine -q` (first run) | 14 passed, 1 failed: `test_input_order_does_not_change_results`. A header-invalid read of block 1 lying just before a valid read of block 1 was left unplaced, because placement required the raw block number to lie strictly between neighbours. |
| Fix: placement bound made inclusive, with the upper bound limited to one past the last valid block when nothing valid follows (so a corrupted raw block number cannot inflate the block range) | n/a |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/engine -q` | `15 passed in 0.24s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `272 passed in 1.67s` |

Tests cover: `RecoverOptions` is the models class; single intact capture (program payload concatenates); lowest flagged block ends the range; missing block; header-invalid-only block (raw-header placement and neighbour-gap placement); empty valid block is `Intact` with `b""`; single-capture CRC failure → `CRC failed`/`single_read`; supporting IDs list every intact capture; Requirement 8.5 case (block 4 from A, block 6 from B); multi-capture failure carries no payload; duplicate capture ID collapse (`duplicates_merged == (A,)`, same blocks, still `single_read`); all 120 permutations of one capture's reads × both capture orders give equal `RecoveryResult`s; empty input raises `ValueError`; an AST import check that no engine module imports `taperescue.decoder_adapter`, `wavcheck`, `fixtures`, `uef`, `server`, or `subprocess`.
