## Task 16.2: End-to-end integration tests
Requirements: 10.7, 14.1, 14.2, 14.3, 14.4, 16.3
### Changes
- `tests/integration/test_end_to_end.py` (new, 21 tests). The module is skipped with a message naming `scripts/build_decoder.sh` when `build/decoder/ScanTape` is missing or not executable, the same pattern as `test_clean_uef.py`. A module-scoped fixture hashes the five inputs (`clean.wav`, `damaged_a.wav`, `damaged_b.wav`, `reference_payloads.json`, `damage.json`) and then decodes the three WAVs with the real patched decoder through `decode_capture`. CLI tests run `[sys.executable, "-m", "taperescue.cli", ...]` as a subprocess with `--decoder build/decoder/ScanTape` and `TAPERESCUE_DECODER` removed from the environment. Expected values come from `damage.json` and `reference_payloads.json`, which the fixture encoder writes without the decoder.
- Tests, by requirement:
  - Decoder observations: the capture IDs equal `damage.json` `wav_sha256`. In each damaged capture, block 6 has a valid header, a complete 256-byte payload, data CRC outcome `invalid` with recorded value 35417 (0x8A59, equal to `damage.json` `data_crc` and the reference block 6 `data_crc`), and differs from the reference payload exactly at the `damage.json` offsets with the recorded original and damaged values.
  - 14.1: each damaged capture alone gives blocks 0 to 5 and 7 `Intact` and block 6 `CRC failed` with reason `single_read`, no payload and no reconstruction. The gate refuses with blockers `((6, "single_read"),)` and `build_uef` raises `ExportRefused`.
  - 14.2: the intact-only merge (`RecoverOptions(reconstruct=False)`) has anchors 0 to 5 and 7. Block 6 is `CRC failed` with no payload and no reconstruction, the other blocks are `Intact`, `program_payload()` is `None`, and the gate refuses with `((6, "crc_failed"),)`.
  - 14.3: full recovery gives block 6 `Reconstructed · CRC match` and blocks 0 to 5 and 7 `Intact`, final block 7, export permitted with `reconstructed_blocks == (6,)`. Block 6 evaluated 16 of 16 candidates with 1 match. Its payload equals the reference block. The choices are exactly offsets 17, 64, 143 and 201. At each offset the damaged capture observed the `damage.json` damaged value and the other capture observed the original; the chosen value is the original and the only supporting capture is the other one. So 17 and 143 come from capture B and 64 and 201 from capture A.
  - Order: `recover([b, a])` gives byte-identical `dumps_report(build_report(...))` text and `build_uef` bytes to `recover([a, b])`.
  - 14.4: `export_uef` writes the image and `read_uef` (the independent reader) reads blocks 0 to 7 back. The concatenated payload equals the reference block hex joined, is 2025 bytes, has SHA-256 `program_sha256`, and equals `program_payload()`. For every block, both CRCs recompute from the file bytes (`header_crc_ok`, `data_crc_ok`). The header bytes, header CRC and data CRC equal the reference values and also equal the values recorded in the block's observation in both damaged captures. Block 6's data CRC in the file is 35417. The image bytes equal the image exported from `clean.wav` alone.
  - CLI full recovery: `recover A B --report --uef` and `recover B A --report --uef` both exit 0 with empty stderr. Stdout shows block 6 reconstructed, export permitted, the reconstructed-block notice, and the reference `program_sha256`. The two runs give byte-identical report files and byte-identical images, and the images equal the clean-path image. In the report, block statuses are as above, `recovered_payload_sha256` equals the reference, the input digests are the two damaged WAVs, block 6 `recorded_data_crc` is 35417, there are 16 candidates and 1 match, and each choice's `chosen` and `supporting_capture_ids` match `damage.json` (offsets in A sourced from B, offsets in B sourced from A). `verify-uef` on the image exits 0 with `verified: 8 blocks match the reference`. `recover clean.wav --uef` gives the same image bytes and prints `reconstructed blocks in this image: none`.
  - 16.3, CLI failure paths (each run as a subprocess; each asserts the exit code, the expected reason in stderr, a last stderr line of the form `error: <reason>`, that no image was written, and that the five input digests are unchanged):

    | Case | Exit | Reason checked in stderr |
    | --- | --- | --- |
    | `recover damaged_a.wav --uef` | 4 | `error: export refused: block 6: single_read` |
    | `recover damaged_b.wav --uef` | 4 | `error: export refused: block 6: single_read` |
    | `recover A B --intact-only --report --uef` | 4 | `error: export refused: block 6: crc_failed`; the report is still written with `reconstruction_enabled: false`, block 6 `CRC failed`, `attempted: false`, export not permitted, no `recovered_payload_sha256` |
    | 22050 Hz mono 16-bit WAV plus A | 3 | `22050 Hz, 16-bit, 1 channel; TapeRescue supports 44100 Hz` |
    | A plus a missing file | 3 | `error: capture not found:` |
    | `--decoder` naming a missing file | 6 | `error: decoder not found at` |
    | three captures | 2 | `error: recover accepts one or two captures (got 3)` |
    | `--uef` naming an input WAV | 2 | `error: the UEF output must not be the input capture` |
  - 10.7: the last test re-hashes the five inputs. The digests equal the ones taken before decoding and the values in `damage.json` and `reference_payloads.json`.

## Task 17.1: Baseline benchmark and `taperescue benchmark`
Requirements: 15.1, 15.2, 15.3, 15.4
### Changes
- `taperescue/benchmark.py` (new):
  - `run_modes(captures, names, reference_sha256)` runs three in-process modes on the same decoded captures. `best_single_capture` recovers each capture alone and keeps the one with the most `Intact` blocks, with ties going to the lowest capture ID. `intact_only_merge` uses `RecoverOptions(reconstruct=False)`. `taperescue` is full recovery. Each mode reports the recovered block count (`Intact` or `Reconstructed · CRC match` among blocks `0..final`), `complete_program` (every required block recovered and the export gate permits export), `matches_reference` (recovered program SHA-256 equals the reference `program_sha256`; `null` when no program was produced), the reconstructed block numbers, and each unrecovered block with its status and reason.
  - `quadbike_comparison(wavs)` runs only when `php` is on `PATH`, `QUADBIKE_LSBLOCKS` names `lsblocks.php`, and `QUADBIKE_BIN` names the Quadbike executable. In that case each WAV is transcribed with `[quadbike, "-t", capture.tibet, wav]`, and then `[php, "-f", lsblocks, "+r", capture.tibet]` runs, both with argument lists and `shell=False` inside a temporary directory. The block rows and the `M: Metrics|Errors|E|Blocks|N` line are parsed from the real stdout. Otherwise the result is `{"ran": false, "reason": ...}` naming every missing piece.
    - Deviation from the task wording `php lsblocks.php +r <wav>`: section 9 of the Quadbike manual gives the usage `php -f lsblocks.php [options] <CSW/TIBET/TIBETZ file>`, and `lsblocks-1.2.php` checks for the CSW magic `Compressed Square Wave`. A WAV cannot be passed directly, so a Quadbike transcription step (`quadbike -t output.tibet input.wav`, from the manual's "Using Quadbike" section) comes first. That step is why `QUADBIKE_BIN` exists.
  - `run_benchmark`, `render_markdown`, `write_results` write `results.json` and `results.md`. The JSON has `"heading": "Inputs: generated STARMAP fixtures"` as its first key. The Markdown file's first line is `# Inputs: generated STARMAP fixtures`. Neither file contains timestamps or absolute paths. The contribution is stated as "reconstruction across captures with byte provenance".
- `taperescue/cli.py`: new `benchmark [--fixtures DIR] [--out DIR] [--decoder PATH] [--timeout S]`. `--fixtures` defaults to `fixtures/starmap` in the repository and `--out` defaults to `benchmarks`. The command validates and decodes `damaged_a.wav` and `damaged_b.wav` with the patched decoder, loads `reference_payloads.json`, and runs inside `_inputs_unchanged` (exit 11 if an input changes). It writes the two result files and prints the table. It reuses the existing exit codes: 3 for a rejected capture, 5 to 8 for decoder failures, 10 for no anchor, and 1 when the output cannot be written. No existing command or exit code changed.
- `tests/test_benchmark.py` (new, minimal): Quadbike absent gives `ran: false` with a reason; `parse_lsblocks` on rows in the manual's format; with the decoder, two `benchmark` runs give byte-identical files, both headed as required, with the mode counts below.
- `verification/pending.md`: P4 stays `pending` with an empty evidence field. It now says why the comparison did not run and how to run it.
- `benchmarks/results.json`, `benchmarks/results.md`: generated by the run below.

### "The star map draws": Pending_Item P2

Not verified. No emulator run was done for this checkpoint, and no result is claimed. The recovered image is byte-identical to the clean image (d), so P1 and P2 test the same bytes, but neither has been run. Steps for the person doing P2:

1. From the repository root, run `.venv/bin/taperescue demo`. It writes `demo-out/STARMAP.uef` (now git-ignored) and `demo-out/report.json`, and must end with `verified: 8 blocks match the reference` and `demo complete: …`.
2. Open https://bbc.xania.org/ (JSBeeb) in a browser.
3. Load `demo-out/STARMAP.uef` with JSBeeb's local tape picker (the cassette option for loading a file from your computer).
4. At the BASIC prompt type `*TAPE` and press Return.
5. Type `CHAIN "STARMAP"` and press Return.
6. Confirm all 8 blocks load with no `Data?` or `Block?` error and the star map draws to completion: the MODE 1 star field, the constellation lines (drawn by `PROCconstellations`, whose bytes are in block 6), and the title.
7. Save a screenshot or screen recording, put its path in P2's evidence field in `verification/pending.md`, and change P2's status. Do the same for P1 with `clean.uef` if it is run.

## Task 19.1: Implement `server/sessions.py`
Requirements: 3.3, 17.2, 17.3
Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue` with Python 3.12.3 (`.venv`). Nothing was committed or pushed. Only `server/`, `tests/server/`, `pyproject.toml` and this entry were changed.
### Changes
- `pyproject.toml`:
  - New optional dependency group `server`: `fastapi>=0.110`, `uvicorn>=0.29`, `python-multipart>=0.0.9`, `httpx>=0.27` (`httpx` is for FastAPI's `TestClient`). The design keeps the server outside the `taperescue` wheel ("installs the `taperescue` wheel and server requirements, copies ..."), so these are optional, not main dependencies.
  - `[tool.pytest.ini_options]`: added `pythonpath = ["."]`, because `server/` is not in the installed package and `--import-mode=importlib` does not put the root on `sys.path`. Without it, `server.sessions` imports only under `python -m pytest`, not bare `pytest`.
- `server/__init__.py` (new): package marker.
- `server/sessions.py` (new):
  - `SessionStore(parent=None, *, clock=time.monotonic, idle_timeout_s=3600, max_upload_bytes=MAX_BYTES)`. The root is a fresh `tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="taperescue-sessions-", dir=parent)` directory, chmod 0700 again in case of an unusual umask. `close()` removes it.
  - `create()` makes `<root>/<uuid4().hex>/` with mode 0700. `get(sid)` and `touch(sid)` update `last_access` from the injected clock. `delete(sid)` removes the directory at once and returns whether the session existed.
  - `sweep()` removes every session with no upload in progress whose idle time is `>= idle_timeout_s` (60 minutes), and returns the sorted IDs it removed. `run_sweeper(interval_s=60)` is a coroutine that runs `sweep` in a worker thread every interval until cancelled, for the FastAPI lifespan in task 19.3.
  - `save_upload_sync(sid, index, chunks, display_name)` and `async save_upload(...)`. The async form accepts an async or a plain iterable of `bytes`. `iter_upload(upload, chunk_size=1 MiB)` turns any object with `async read(n)`, such as `UploadFile`, into an async iterator. Uploads go to `capture_<index>.wav.part` (opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`, mode 0600), with a running byte count. When the count passes `max_upload_bytes` (64 MiB, `taperescue.wavcheck.MAX_BYTES`), the partial file is closed and deleted and `UploadTooLarge` is raised with code `too_large`, `capture_index`, and the message `Capture is larger than the 64 MiB limit (67108864 bytes)`. An upload of exactly the limit is kept. On success the `.part` file is renamed to `capture_0.wav` or `capture_1.wav`.
  - The user filename goes only into `StoredCapture.display_name`, with non-printable characters dropped and a 255-character cap. It is never joined into a path.
  - Session IDs are checked against `[0-9a-f]{32}` with `fullmatch` before any path join. The joined path must also be a direct child of the root, and `_remove_dir` checks the same before `rmtree`. Capture indexes must be the `int` 0 or 1 (`bool`, strings and floats are rejected).
  - The session table is guarded by a `threading.Lock`. A session with an upload in progress is never swept. If a session is deleted during an upload, the upload raises `SessionNotFound` and leaves no files.
  - Typed errors, all subclasses of `SessionError` with `code` and a user-facing `message`: `InvalidSessionId` (`invalid_session_id`), `SessionNotFound` (`not_found`), both with the message `Session not found or expired`; `InvalidCaptureIndex` (`invalid_capture_index`); `UploadTooLarge` (`too_large`).
- `tests/server/test_sessions.py` (new, 35 tests): private 0700 root (given parent and default temp dir, removed by `close`); generated 32-hex IDs and 0700 session directories; generated stored names with a hostile display name (`../../etc/passwd\x00evil.wav`) kept out of paths; sync, async, and async-with-plain-iterable uploads; invalid capture indexes; upload of exactly the limit kept; over-limit abort that stops reading at the crossing chunk and leaves the session directory empty (sync and async); default limit 64 MiB; `last_access` update on `get`; sweep with an injected clock that keeps a session at 59:59 idle, removes it at 60:00, and keeps a session touched 1 s earlier; sweep skipping an in-progress upload; `run_sweeper`; delete on request; upload to a deleted session; 14 invalid session IDs (traversal, wrong length, uppercase, `/`, newline, NUL, non-ASCII digits, `None`, `int`) rejected by `get`, `delete`, `capture_path` and `save_upload_sync` with no change to the root; unknown valid ID gives `SessionNotFound`; 8 threads creating, uploading and deleting 160 sessions.

## Task 19.3: Implement `server/app.py`
Requirements: 3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.4, 3.5, 10.8, 17.1, 17.3, 17.4, 17.5, 19.1, 19.2
Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue` with `.venv` (FastAPI 0.165.0, Starlette 1.10.0, python-multipart 0.0.34, httpx 0.28.1) and the built decoder at `build/decoder/ScanTape`. Nothing was installed, committed or pushed. Only `server/app.py`, `tests/server/test_app.py` and this entry were added.
### Changes
- `server/app.py` (new): `create_app(...)` factory plus the module-level `app` for `uvicorn server.app:app`.
  - Configuration: `TAPERESCUE_DECODER` (default `build/decoder/ScanTape`), `TAPERESCUE_FIXTURES` (default `fixtures/starmap`), `TAPERESCUE_STATIC` (default `web/dist`, mounted at `/` only if it holds `index.html`). Defaults are relative to the repository, not the working directory. The factory also takes a session parent directory, a clock, the decoder timeout (default 60 s) and the sweep interval, for tests.
  - Lifespan: creates the `SessionStore` (private 0700 root), starts a sweep task every 60 s that calls `store.sweep()` in a worker thread and drops the swept sessions' in-memory results, and on shutdown cancels the task and calls `store.close()`.
  - `POST /api/sessions`: reads the multipart form with `max_files=2`. Files in field `captures` are streamed into the session by `SessionStore.save_upload` under `capture_0.wav` / `capture_1.wav`. The user filename is display text only. A `Content-Length` over 2 × 64 MiB + 1 MiB, or a streamed body over that, returns 413. A single upload over 64 MiB becomes a `too_large` capture error.
  - `POST /api/sample`: copies `damaged_a.wav` and `damaged_b.wav` into a new session with `save_upload_sync`, then runs the same pipeline. Returns 503 if the files are missing.
  - Pipeline, run in one `anyio.to_thread.run_sync` call: decoder check (503 if missing or not executable), then for each capture `validate_capture` (wavcheck) and `decode_capture(path, decoder=..., workdir=session dir, timeout_s=60)`, then `recover`, `export_decision`, `build_report`. The report text is `dumps_report(report) + "\n"`, the same bytes the CLI writes.
  - Envelope, matching `SessionEnvelope` in `web/src/api.ts`: `session_id`, `captures` (`index`, `display_name`, `capture_id`, which is `null` for a wavcheck or size rejection, and `status` `"ok"`/`"error"`), `errors` (`capture_index`, `display_name`, `code`, `message`), `report`, `observations` (keyed by capture ID; each block is the `observations_to_doc` shape plus `block_no`, which is `null` for an invalid header), and `export` (`permitted`, `blockers` as `[block_no, code]`, `reconstructed_blocks`).
  - Status rules: 200 when at least one capture decoded. A session with one rejected and one valid capture returns the valid capture's single-capture report, with the rejection in `errors`. 422 `{"errors": [...]}` when no capture decoded, or for `no_anchor` (`capture_index: null`). The session is deleted on 422 because there is nothing to show or download. 400 `{"detail": "Upload one or two WAV captures"}` for zero files, more than two, or non-file values in `captures`. Other malformed multipart bodies get a different 400 message. 404 `{"detail": "Session not found or expired"}` for unknown, expired or malformed IDs.
  - `GET /api/sessions/{sid}` returns the stored envelope. `GET .../report.json` returns the report with `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="taperescue-report.json"`, including for refused sessions (Requirement 10.8). `GET .../tape.uef` returns 409 `{"detail", "blockers", "messages"}` when the gate refuses. Otherwise it runs `export_uef` into `<session>/tape.uef`, which verifies the file with the independent reader, and returns the bytes with `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<cassette name>.uef"`. The name comes from block 0's valid header and must match `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,10}`, otherwise it is `tape`. Verification failure returns 500 `Exported image failed verification`. `DELETE` returns 204, or 404 if the session does not exist. Every lookup goes through `store.get`, which updates `last_access`.
  - A pure ASGI middleware adds `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` to every response, and `Cache-Control: no-store` to `/api/` responses. There is no CORS. OpenAPI and docs routes are disabled. The only process that sees a capture is the local decoder subprocess (argument list, `shell=False`, inside `decode_capture`).
- `tests/server/test_app.py` (new, 12 tests; 5 need the decoder and are skipped with a message when it is missing): 400 for 0 and 3 files; 422 with `capture_index` 0 for a 48 kHz stereo WAV and for non-WAV bytes, using a filename containing `../../etc/`, with no session or files left behind; 404 for unknown and malformed IDs on all four session routes; CSP, nosniff and no-store headers on API and static responses; 503 with a missing decoder. With the decoder: the sample reconstructs block 6 and the downloaded `tape.uef`, read with `read_uef`, equals the reference program and `recovered_payload_sha256`; `report.json` equals `dumps_report(envelope.report) + "\n"`; DELETE removes the session; a single capture gives a 409 with `[[6, "single_read"]]` while `report.json` still downloads; one valid capture plus one 8-bit capture gives 200 with the error on index 1; a silent WAV gives 422 `no_blocks`; the sweep with an injected clock keeps a session touched within 60 minutes and removes it, including its directory, 60 minutes after the last request.

Fixed by catching `starlette.exceptions.HTTPException`. (2) The test ID `..%2F..` was normalised to `/` by httpx before it reached the server, so the static index answered. ### Notes for later tasks
- Tasks 20.x (UI): a 422 deletes the session, so the UI shows `errors` from `ApiError.captureErrors` and has no session to fetch. A 200 can carry `errors` for one rejected capture next to a report for the other.
- Task 19.4 (API tests) can extend `tests/server/test_app.py`. `no_anchor` (422, `capture_index: null`) is implemented but not tested yet, because it needs two decodable captures of different programs.
- Starlette spools multipart files to an anonymous `SpooledTemporaryFile` (memory up to 1 MiB, then the system temp dir) before `save_upload` copies them into the session and enforces 64 MiB per capture. The whole request body is capped at 2 × 64 MiB + 1 MiB while it streams.

None.

## Task 20.1: Vite React TypeScript scaffold and API client
Requirements: 19.1
Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue/web` with Node v22.19.0 and npm 10.9.3, both already installed. No system software was installed. Nothing was committed or pushed. Only `web/` and this entry were added.
### Changes (all new, under `web/`)
- `package.json`: `private`, `"type": "module"`, `engines.node` `^22.12.0 || >=24.0.0`, exact versions (no ranges). Scripts: `dev` (`vite`), `build` (`tsc -b && vite build`), `typecheck`, `preview`, `test` (`vitest --run`).
  - Dependencies: `react` 19.3.1, `react-dom` 19.3.1.
  - Dev dependencies: `vite` 8.3.8, `@vitejs/plugin-react` 6.3.0, `typescript` 5.9.3, `@types/react` 19.3.0, `@types/react-dom` 19.3.0, `@types/node` 22.20.5, `vitest` 5.1.1, `@testing-library/react` 16.3.3, `@testing-library/dom` 10.4.2, `jsdom` 29.1.1.
  - Version choices: these are the current npm releases except two. `jsdom` 30 and 31 require Node `^22.22.2`, and this machine has 22.19.0, so 29.1.1 (`^22.13.0`) is used. `typescript` 7.0.2 is the new native compiler, so 5.9.3, the last 5.x release, is used for a standard `tsc -b`.
- `package-lock.json`: lockfileVersion 3, 98 packages, every `resolved` URL on `registry.npmjs.org`. SHA-256 `03bc8620…a4678`. `npm install` reported `found 0 vulnerabilities`.
- `vite.config.ts`: React plugin. The dev server runs on `127.0.0.1:5173` (`strictPort`) and proxies `/api` to `http://127.0.0.1:8000`. The build sets `assetsInlineLimit: 0` so no asset is inlined as a `data:` URI, because the CSP `default-src 'self'` would block one. Vitest uses the `jsdom` environment by default, for task 20.5.
- `tsconfig.json` with `tsconfig.app.json` (src; strict, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `resolveJsonModule`) and `tsconfig.node.json` (vite.config.ts) as project references.
- `index.html`: references only `/src/main.tsx`. It has no favicon, font, CDN or analytics links.
- `src/main.tsx`, `src/index.css` (system font stack only, no `@font-face` or `@import`), and a placeholder `src/App.tsx` (title and one line of text, no network calls).
- `src/api.ts`, the typed client:
  - The report types mirror `build_report` in `taperescue/engine/report.py`: `Report` (`schema`, `algorithm_version`, `candidate_cap`, `reconstruction_enabled`, `inputs`, `duplicates_merged`, `anchors`, `final_block`, `blocks`, `export`, optional `recovered_payload_sha256`), `ReportInput`, `ReportBlock`, `ReportRead`, `ReconstructionEntry`, `ReconstructionChoice` (with `observed` as `Record<capture_id, number | number[]>`), `ConflictEntry`, `ReportExport` (`permitted`, `blockers`, `messages`, `reconstructed_blocks`).
  - Code unions: `BlockStatus`, `CrcOutcome`, `PayloadState`, `UnresolvedReason`, `GateReason`, and `ReasonCode` (equal to the keys of `REASON_MESSAGES`).
  - The envelope follows design.md section 11: `SessionEnvelope` (`session_id`, `captures`, `errors`, `report`, `observations`, `export`), `CaptureSummary`, `ObservationBlock` (the shape `observations_to_doc` writes, plus optional `block_no`), and `ExportDecision`.
  - Error models: `CaptureError` (`capture_index`, `display_name`, `code`, `message`, as in the design's 422 body), `CaptureErrorCode` (wavcheck, decoder and `no_anchor` codes), `CaptureErrorsBody`, `ExportRefusedBody`, and `ApiError` (`status`, `captureErrors`, `blockers`). `readApiError` reads `errors`, `blockers`, `message`, or FastAPI's string `detail`, and falls back to the design's fixed messages for 400, 404, 409 and 422.
  - Fetch helpers: `createSession(files)` (`POST /api/sessions`, multipart field `captures`; rejects zero or more than two files before sending), `runSample()` (`POST /api/sample`), `getSession(sid)`, `fetchReportFile(sid)` (`report.json`), `fetchTapeFile(sid)` (`tape.uef`; a 409 rejects with `ApiError.blockers`), `deleteSession(sid)`, plus `sessionUrl`, `reportUrl` and `tapeUrl`. Every URL is same-origin under `/api`, and the session ID is passed through `encodeURIComponent`.
- `src/api.test.ts` and `src/test-fixtures/report.json`, `report-single.json`: the fixtures are real reports written by `.venv/bin/taperescue recover fixtures/starmap/damaged_a.wav fixtures/starmap/damaged_b.wav --report …` (exit 0, export permitted, block 6 reconstructed) and by the same command with `damaged_a.wav` alone (exit 0, blocker `block 6: single_read`). Neither contains `/tmp` or any other path. The test compares the keys of every report object with key tables typed `Record<keyof T, true>`. `tsc` fails if a table and its interface differ, and the test fails if a real report and a table differ. It also tests `readApiError` on real `Response` objects for 422, 409, 404 and non-JSON bodies, and checks the URL helpers.
### Server contract points for task 19.3
`server/` does not exist yet, so these envelope details come from the design and the client defines them. Task 19.3 should either match them or update `api.ts`:
- `CaptureSummary.status` is `"ok" | "error"`. The design only shows `"ok"`.
- `capture_id` may be `null` for a rejected capture.
- A session-level error such as `no_anchor` uses `capture_index: null`.
- `report` and `export` are `null` when no capture decoded.
- 409 bodies carry `blockers`. 400 and 404 errors carry a string `detail` or `message`.

## Task 20.2: Capture and merged strips
Requirements: 18.1, 18.2
Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue/web` with the toolchain from task 20.1 (Node v22.19.0). No dependency was added. `App.tsx` is not wired (task 20.4). Nothing was committed or pushed. Only the files below and this entry were added.
### Changes (all new, under `web/src/`)
- `status.ts`
  - `BLOCK_STATUS_STYLES`: label, background colour and text colour per `BlockStatus`, from the design table: Intact `#2e7d32`, CRC failed `#c62828`, Reconstructed (for `Reconstructed · CRC match`) `#1565c0`, Unresolved `#ef6c00`, Conflict `#6a1b9a`, Missing `#616161`. Text is white except on amber, which uses `#1a1a1a` because white on `#ef6c00` is about 3.1:1, below the 4.5:1 WCAG AA threshold for small text.
  - `CaptureReadStatus` and `CAPTURE_READ_STYLES` for per-capture cells, derived from the report's per-block `reads` for that capture: `Intact` (header CRC valid, and the payload is empty or complete with a valid data CRC), `CRC failed` (complete, data CRC invalid), `CRC unreadable`, `Truncated`, `Header failed` (header CRC not valid), and `Missing` (no read placed at the block). Colours reuse the design tokens: green for a good read, red for a read failing a CRC check, amber for missing evidence (truncated or unreadable CRC), grey for no read.
  - `readStatus(read)` and `captureReadStatus(block, captureId)`. When a capture read a block more than once, the best read is shown (Intact > CRC failed > CRC unreadable > Truncated > Header failed) and the read count goes into the accessible name.
- `CaptureStrip.tsx`
  - `BlockStrip`, the shared row: a `<section>` labelled by an `<h2>` (so it is a named `region`) containing an `<ol role="list">` with one `<button type="button">` per block. Each button shows the block number and the text label, sets `aria-pressed` for the selected block, has an `aria-label` such as `Block 6: CRC failed` that contains the visible text, and a `title` tooltip. Native buttons give keyboard operation (Tab, Enter, Space). Colours are applied through React's `style` prop, which React sets through the CSSOM, so the API's CSP `default-src 'self'` does not block them.
  - `CaptureStrip({report, captureId, title?, selectedBlock, onSelect})`: one cell per `report.blocks` entry. The heading is the upload's display name with the first 8 hex digits of the capture ID, or `Capture <id8>` when no name is given.
- `MergedStrip.tsx`: `MergedStrip({report, title = "Merged result", selectedBlock, onSelect})`. The cell shows the merged status label. The accessible name carries the full status (`Block 6: Reconstructed · CRC match`), and the tooltip shows `reason_message`, else `label`.
- `strips.css`: local styles only (no `url(`, `@import` or remote reference). The selected cell gets a 3 px dark outline, focused cells get a `:focus-visible` outline, and cells wrap on narrow screens.
- `status.test.ts`, `strips.test.tsx` (co-located, like `api.test.ts`), using the real reports in `src/test-fixtures/`.

```
✓ src/strips.test.tsx > selection > reports the clicked block and marks only that cell pressed in every strip
```

## Task 20.3: Block inspector

Requirements: 18.3

Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue/web` with the toolchain from task 20.1. No dependency was added. `App.tsx` is not wired (task 20.4). Nothing was committed or pushed. Only the files below and this entry were added.

### Changes (all new, under `web/src/`)

- `BlockInspector.tsx`: `BlockInspector({report, blockNo, displayNames?})`. `blockNo` is the selected block, or `null` when nothing is selected. `displayNames` maps capture ID to upload name; a capture without a name shows the first 8 hex digits of its ID (`shortId` from `CaptureStrip.tsx`). The component renders a `<section>` labelled by an `<h2>` (`Block N`) containing:
  - A `<dl>` with the status (text plus the status colour from `status.ts`). It also holds the report's `label` as "Decision", the report's `reason_message` as "Reason" when one is set, and the block's recorded data CRC as `0x8A59`-style hex. For Intact and Reconstructed blocks it adds "Supplied by" with the capture names from `supporting_capture_ids`. When reconstruction was attempted it adds candidate, evaluated and CRC match counts. For a conflict it adds the conflict kind and capture names. All decision text comes from the report (`STATUS_LABELS` and `REASON_MESSAGES` in `taperescue/engine/report.py`). The component adds no explanatory strings of its own.
  - A reads table (`<caption>Reads of block N by capture</caption>`, `<th scope="col">` headers, one `<th scope="row">` per read). Columns: capture, sample offset, header CRC outcome, data CRC outcome, recorded data CRC in hex, expected and actual length, and payload state. A capture listed in `report.inputs` with no read of the block gets a row saying "No read of this block".
  - A differing-offsets table, shown only when the report has choices for the block. Columns: offset, then each capture's observed value as `0x61 (97)`. Several values from one capture are listed comma-separated, and a capture with no value shows "Not observed". The Chosen and Supplied by columns are rendered only for `Reconstructed · CRC match`. Other statuses never show chosen bytes, even if the data carried them.
  - `formatByte` and `formatCrc` are exported for reuse.
- `inspector.css`: local styles only (no `url(`, `@import` or remote reference). Wide tables scroll inside a wrapper `div`, because `display: block` on the table itself can drop its table role for screen readers.
- `BlockInspector.test.tsx`: uses the real reports in `src/test-fixtures/`.

### Evidence

| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `npm run build` (`tsc -b && vite build`) | Exit 0 on the first attempt. The bundle is unchanged (`index-DoF1K4LA.js`, 219.75 kB) because `App.tsx` does not import the inspector yet |
| `npm test` (`vitest --run`) | `Test Files 4 passed (4)`, `Tests 24 passed (24)` on the first attempt. 17 existing tests, 7 new |

New tests (`npx vitest --run --reporter=verbose`):

```
✓ src/BlockInspector.test.tsx > formatting > shows bytes in hex and decimal and CRCs as 4-digit hex
✓ src/BlockInspector.test.tsx > BlockInspector, two damaged captures > shows block 6 reads, recorded CRC, differing offsets, chosen values and evidence label
✓ src/BlockInspector.test.tsx > BlockInspector, two damaged captures > shows which captures supplied intact block 0, with no offsets table
✓ src/BlockInspector.test.tsx > BlockInspector, two damaged captures > falls back to a short capture ID prefix without display names
✓ src/BlockInspector.test.tsx > BlockInspector, two damaged captures > prompts for a selection when no block is selected
✓ src/BlockInspector.test.tsx > BlockInspector, unrecovered blocks > shows CRC failed and the single_read reason for block 6 of one capture
✓ src/BlockInspector.test.tsx > BlockInspector, unrecovered blocks > never shows chosen values for an Unresolved block, even if the data carries them
```

What the tests check against the real reports (capture `2e09…` is `damaged_a.wav` and `83a0…` is `damaged_b.wav`):

- Two-capture report, block 6: recorded data CRC `0x8A59`. The Decision text is the report label `CRC match is evidence, not proof of the original bytes`, with 16 candidates, 16 evaluated and 1 match. Both reads show offset 1116172, header CRC valid, data CRC invalid, `0x8A59`, 256/256, complete. The offsets table reads:

  | Offset | A | B | Chosen | Supplied by |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 17 | 0x65 (101) | 0x61 (97) | 0x61 (97) | damaged_b.wav |
  | 64 | 0xE0 (224) | 0xE1 (225) | 0xE0 (224) | damaged_a.wav |
  | 143 | 0x14 (20) | 0x34 (52) | 0x34 (52) | damaged_b.wav |
  | 201 | 0x69 (105) | 0x29 (41) | 0x69 (105) | damaged_a.wav |

  The column headers are `scope="col"` and the row headers `scope="row"`. The damaged offsets match the plan: A was damaged at 17 and 143, so B supplies those bytes, and B was damaged at 64 and 201, so A supplies those.
- Two-capture report, block 0: "Supplied by" reads `damaged_a.wav, damaged_b.wav`. The block shows the Intact label, both data CRCs are valid, and it has no offsets table and no candidate counts.
- Single-capture report, block 6: the status is `CRC failed`, the Reason is the report's `single_read` message, and the recorded CRC is `0x8A59`. It has no "Supplied by" and no offsets table, and the reads table has one row for capture A.
- Block 6 recast as `Unresolved`, with the chosen values left in the data: the offsets table has only Offset, A and B columns, and no "Chosen" text appears anywhere.
- Without display names, rows are labelled with the 8-character ID prefix. With `blockNo={null}`, the inspector shows a prompt and no tables.

### Notes

- Task 20.4 wires the inspector into `App.tsx`. It passes the same `selectedBlock` used by the strips as `blockNo`, and builds `displayNames` from the session envelope's `captures` (`capture_id` → `display_name`).

## Task 20.4: Implement downloads, JSBeeb help, and wire the app
Requirements: 11.6, 18.4, 18.5, 18.6, 18.7
Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue` with Node packages already in `web/node_modules`, `.venv`, and the built decoder at `build/decoder/ScanTape`. Nothing was installed, committed or pushed. Source changes are only under `web/src/`; `web/dist` was rebuilt by `npm run build`.
### Changes
- `web/src/DownloadBar.tsx` (new): **Download report** is always enabled, including for refused sessions. **Download tape image** is `disabled` while `export.permitted` is false. The blockers are listed beside it as `Block N: <message> <code>`, with the message taken from `report.export.messages`, and the list is linked to the button with `aria-describedby`. When the image contains reconstructed blocks, clicking opens a native modal `<dialog>` (`showModal()`, falling back to the `open` attribute where `showModal` is missing, as in jsdom). The dialog lists each reconstructed block as `Block 6: Reconstructed · CRC match. CRC match is evidence, not proof of the original bytes`, with the label taken from the report. Nothing is fetched until **Continue download** is pressed. Focus moves to that button when the dialog opens. Escape or **Cancel** closes it and returns focus to the tape button. A 409 on download replaces the shown blockers with the server's and disables the button. Other errors appear in a `role="alert"` line. Fetch and save functions can be passed in as props; the defaults are `fetchReportFile` / `fetchTapeFile` from `api.ts` and `saveBlob`.
- `web/src/download.ts` (new): `saveBlob(blob, filename)` creates a `blob:` object URL, clicks a hidden `<a download>`, removes it and revokes the URL after 1 s. `safeCassetteName` applies the server's `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,10}` rule.
- `web/src/JsBeebHelp.tsx` (new): a plain outbound link to `https://bbc.xania.org/` (`target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`), with no iframe or embed. Steps: download the `.uef`, open JSBeeb, load the file with the emulator's local tape picker (the page states that TapeRescue does not send it to JSBeeb), type `*TAPE`, then `CHAIN "STARMAP"`. The name comes from the session's cassette name when known and defaults to `STARMAP`.
- `web/src/App.tsx` (replaces the placeholder):
  - A file input (`multiple`, WAV accept list) with a **Recover** button, enabled only for one or two files. Selecting three or more shows an alert.
  - **Try sample** calls `runSample()`.
  - Each upload is listed with its display name, "decoded" or "rejected", and its error messages. The messages come from `envelope.errors` on a 200, or from `ApiError.captureErrors` on a 422, when no envelope exists. Session-level errors (`capture_index: null`, for example `no_anchor`) are listed separately. Any other `ApiError` message (400, 404, 503) is shown as one alert.
  - One `CaptureStrip` per `report.inputs` entry, titled with the upload display name (two uploads with the same ID are joined with ` / `). Then `MergedStrip`, `BlockInspector` (with display names) and `DownloadBar`. The tape file name is `<cassette name>.uef`, taken from a valid block 0 header in the observations.
  - Selection defaults to the first merged block that is not `Intact` (`defaultBlock`), which is block 6 in the sample.
  - When a new session replaces the old one, the app sends `DELETE` for the old session and ignores any failure.
- `web/src/downloads.css` (new): styles for the buttons, blockers, dialog and its `::backdrop`, the upload row and the JSBeeb section. There are no inline `<style>` tags. Cell colours are still set through React's `style` prop, which uses the CSSOM.
- `web/src/downloads.test.tsx` (new, 3 tests), using the recorded report fixtures:
  - Damaged A alone: the tape button is disabled, the blocker reads `Block 6: <single_read message> single_read`, clicking the tape button fetches nothing, and the report downloads as `taperescue-report.json`.
  - Both captures: the dialog lists block 6 with the evidence label, focus is on **Continue download**, Cancel closes the dialog without fetching and returns focus, and confirming saves the tape blob as `STARMAP.uef`.
  - JsBeebHelp: the link `href`, `target` and `rel`; the local tape picker, `*TAPE` and `CHAIN "STARMAP"` steps; no iframe.

## Task 21.1: Write the `Dockerfile` and `.dockerignore`
Requirements: 17.6, 19.3, 19.4
Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue` with Docker 29.1.3 (`docker info` works without sudo). The image was built and tagged locally as `taperescue:local` and left in the local image store. Nothing was pushed, committed, or installed into `.venv`. Only `Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore` and this entry were added. `pyproject.toml` was not changed.
### Changes
- `Dockerfile` (new, 102 lines), four stages:
  - `decoder` (`debian:bookworm-slim`): installs `g++ make cmake git zlib1g-dev`, copies only `third_party/`, `patches/` and `scripts/build_decoder.sh`, then runs the build script. The script checks the vendored revision and tree id, applies the patch with `git apply`, and builds `ScanTape`. It runs offline because the vendored tree is present. The stage then checks that the usage text lists `-json <file>` (the patched option). It reads `ScanTape`'s exit 255 without using a pipe, so hadolint DL4006 does not apply.
  - `web` (`node:22-bookworm-slim`): `npm ci`, then `npm run build` (`tsc -b && vite build`). **This differs from the task text (node 20).** `web/package.json` declares `"engines": {"node": "^22.12.0 || >=24.0.0"}` because Vite 8 requires it, so Node 20 is outside the supported range. The local toolchain is Node 22.19.0.
  - `wheel` (`python:3.12-slim-bookworm`): `pip wheel --no-deps .` with `pyproject.toml`, `LICENSE`, `TapeRescue-Kiro-Brief.md` (the `readme`) and `taperescue/`.
  - `runtime` (`python:3.12-slim-bookworm`): installs `zlib1g libstdc++6` for ScanTape's dynamic links, so glibc and libstdc++ come from the same Debian release as the decoder stage. It creates system user/group `taperescue` with uid/gid 10001 and installs `taperescue-0.1.0-*.whl[server]` with the server dependencies pinned to the versions the API tests ran on: `fastapi==0.165.0`, `starlette==1.10.0`, `uvicorn==0.54.0`, `python-multipart==0.0.34`, `anyio==4.16.0`. It then copies `server/{__init__,app,sessions}.py` to `/app/server/`, `ScanTape` and `BUILD_INFO` to `/app/bin/`, `web/dist` to `/app/web/dist/`, and `clean.wav`, `damaged_a.wav` and `damaged_b.wav` to `/app/fixtures/starmap/`. The process runs as `USER 10001:10001` (numeric IDs, hadolint DL3066) with `EXPOSE 8000` and `CMD ["uvicorn", "server.app:app", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000"]`. Files under `/app` are owned by root and not writable by the app user.
- `.dockerignore` (new): excludes `.git`, `.gitignore`, `.venv`, `build`, `**/node_modules`, `web/dist`, `*.egg-info`, `demo-out`, `**/__pycache__`, `**/*.py[cod]`, `.pytest_cache`, `.hypothesis`, `**/*.rom`/`*.ROM`/`*.Rom`, `**/jsbeeb`, `**/jsbeeb-*` and `**/JSBeeb`. Docker's patterns are case-sensitive, so `web/src/JsBeebHelp.tsx` stays in the context. Nothing in `third_party/` matches a pattern, so the build script's tree-id check still passes.
### Findings from the code
- Server paths (`server/app.py` lines 99–107): `REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]`. The defaults are `build/decoder/ScanTape`, `fixtures/starmap` and `web/dist` under that root, and env vars `TAPERESCUE_DECODER`, `TAPERESCUE_FIXTURES` and `TAPERESCUE_STATIC` override them. The image sets all three explicitly: `TAPERESCUE_DECODER=/app/bin/ScanTape`, `TAPERESCUE_FIXTURES=/app/fixtures/starmap`, `TAPERESCUE_STATIC=/app/web/dist`. It also sets `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` and `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1`.
- Packaging: `[tool.setuptools.packages.find] include = ["taperescue", "taperescue.*"]`, so `server/` is not in the wheel. The Dockerfile copies it as source into `/app`, and `/app` is the working directory, so `server.app` imports from there.
- Dependencies: the `server` extra already declares `fastapi`, `uvicorn` and `python-multipart` (plus `httpx`). `anyio` and `starlette`, which `server/` imports directly, come in through FastAPI. Nothing was missing, so `pyproject.toml` is unchanged.
- Sessions use `tempfile.mkdtemp` in `/tmp`, which the non-root user can write to. In the running container, the session root was `drwx------ taperescue taperescue /tmp/taperescue-sessions-b7ljefra`.
### Evidence
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `docker --version`; `docker info` | `Docker version 29.1.3`; `docker info` exit 0 without sudo |
| `docker run --rm -i hadolint/hadolint:latest-debian hadolint - < Dockerfile` (image pulled for linting only) | First run: DL3008 ×2, DL4006 (pipe in the decoder check), DL3066 (non-numeric `USER`). Fixed DL4006 and DL3066. Final run: only DL3008 ×2 ("pin versions in apt-get install"). These are left on purpose because bookworm point releases replace package versions, and exact apt pins would break the build |
| `docker build -t taperescue:local .` | Exit 0, image `sha256:0ac600e540b3…`, 160 MB. Decoder stage: `verified vendored source is revision 98d1a8d1… (tree faf78c9b…)`, `applied /src/patches/atomtapeutils-json-observations.patch`, `built /src/build/decoder/ScanTape`, `BUILD_INFO` `patch_sha256=ba59709a…c628` (same as the local build). Web stage: `✓ built in 424ms`, no `EBADENGINE` warnings. Runtime pip: `Successfully installed … fastapi-0.165.0 … python-multipart-0.0.34 starlette-1.10.0 taperescue-0.1.0 … uvicorn-0.54.0` |

### Notes for later tasks
- Task 21.2 (`tests/smoke/test_container.py`) can repeat the `docker export … | tar -t` scan used above. Building takes about 1–2 minutes with a cold cache, so the test should skip when Docker is unavailable.
- README (task 23.x): `docker build -t taperescue .` then `docker run --rm -p 8000:8000 taperescue`. The UI is at `http://127.0.0.1:8000/`.
- Base images use tags (`debian:bookworm-slim`, `node:22-bookworm-slim`, `python:3.12-slim-bookworm`), not digests, so rebuilds pick up Debian security updates. Pinning digests would make builds fully reproducible.
- Deployment to a public URL is still a Pending_Item. This task only built and ran the image locally.

None. One deviation: the web stage uses Node 22, not Node 20, because `web/package.json` requires Node `^22.12.0 || >=24.0.0`.

## Task 23.1: Create the recovery test hook
Requirements: 20.1, 20.2
### Changes
- `.kiro/hooks/recovery-tests.json` (new). One hook in the v1 format used by `.kiro/hooks/auto-commit-docs-on-major-change.json` (`version`, `hooks[]` with `name`, `trigger`, `description`, `action`), plus `matcher` and `timeout`:
  - `trigger`: `PostFileSave`
  - `matcher`: `(^|/)taperescue/(decoder_adapter|engine)/.+\.py$` (regex against the saved file path; matches relative and absolute paths, including subdirectories)
  - `action`: `{"type": "command", "command": ".venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/engine tests/decoder_adapter tests/integration -q && .venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/properties -q"}`
  - `timeout`: 180 seconds (the default is 60; the command takes about 40 s)
- `.kiro/hooks/auto-commit-docs-on-major-change.json` was not modified (`git diff --stat` on it is empty).

### Schema source
[Kiro hooks overview](https://kiro.dev/docs/hooks/) and [Hook triggers](https://kiro.dev/docs/hooks/types/), fetched during this task. The overview gives the v1 schema: `hooks[].trigger` (PascalCase), optional `matcher` (for file events, a regex against the file path), `action.type` `"command"` or `"agent"`, `action.command` for command actions, optional `timeout` in seconds (default 60). Command actions run in the project root and receive the event JSON on STDIN. The trigger names in the task text (`PostFileSave`, `matcher`) match the documented schema, so no renaming was needed. A command action was chosen over an agent prompt so the tests run directly instead of asking the agent to run them.

### Choice of test directories
The design names `tests/engine tests/decoder_adapter`. Coverage of the matched source was checked with `grep` for imports of `taperescue.engine` and `taperescue.decoder_adapter` under `tests/`:
- `tests/engine` (8 files): engine unit and acceptance tests. Included.
- `tests/decoder_adapter/test_schema.py`: schema validation only. Included.
- `tests/integration` (3 files): the only tests that run `taperescue/decoder_adapter/run.py` (`decode_capture` with the built `ScanTape`, truncated-WAV and silent-WAV cases in `test_fixture_decode.py`), plus the clean-fixture UEF path and the STARMAP end-to-end recovery through the engine. Included, so a change to `run.py` is actually exercised. These skip with a message if `build/decoder/ScanTape` is missing.
- `tests/properties` (6 files): every file imports `taperescue.engine` (recover, reconstruct, gate, report). Included.
- Not included: `tests/server`, `tests/smoke` and the top-level `tests/test_*.py` files. They test the server, CLI, fixture encoder, UEF and WAV checks, which are outside the matched directories.

### Pre-existing finding: property generator-coverage checks depend on which tests run together
A single pytest run over the chosen directories fails one generator-coverage test, while the same test passes alone, with `tests/properties` alone, and in the full suite. Hypothesis is 6.170.17.
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/engine tests/decoder_adapter tests/properties tests/integration -q` | `1 failed, 190 passed in 40.86s`: `test_prop_order_independence.py::test_generator_reaches_interesting_cases`, `('status:Missing', ...) assert 4 >= 5`. Reproduced on a second run |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/engine tests/decoder_adapter tests/properties -q` | `1 failed, 159 passed in 39.29s`: `test_prop_uef_roundtrip.py::test_generator_covers_the_cases` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/properties/test_prop_order_independence.py::test_generator_reaches_interesting_cases -q` | `1 passed in 3.41s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/properties -q` | `15 passed in 36.39s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `489 passed, 1 warning in 46.18s` |

Both failing tests use `derandomize=True` with fixed minimum counts per case. The likely cause is that this Hypothesis version mixes constants collected from the imported local modules into generation (the `.hypothesis/constants/` directory), so the derandomized sample changes with the set of modules loaded. That cause has not been confirmed. The tests and the engine were not changed in this task. The hook runs `tests/properties` in its own pytest invocation, which passes and is deterministic on the current code.

### Commands and results
Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue`, decoder present at `build/decoder/ScanTape`.
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Python check: load the hook JSON, print trigger/action type/timeout, test the matcher with `re.search` | `PostFileSave command 180`. Matches `taperescue/engine/reconstruct.py`, `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue/taperescue/engine/gate.py`, `taperescue/decoder_adapter/run.py`, `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue/taperescue/decoder_adapter/schema.py`, `taperescue/engine/sub/x.py`. Does not match `taperescue/uef/exporter.py`, `tests/engine/test_gate.py`, `taperescue/engine/notes.md`, `taperescue/engine/gate.pyc`, `server/app.py`, `other_taperescue/engine/x.py` |
| Hook command read from the JSON and run with `bash -c` from the project root, with `{"hook_event_name":"PostFileSave"}` on STDIN | Exit status 0. `176 passed in 3.13s`, then `15 passed in 34.17s`. An earlier identical run gave `176 passed in 3.19s` and `15 passed in 36.52s`, about 41 s wall time |

### Blockers
- The hook firing inside Kiro on a file save was not observed. The docs say hooks load when a session starts, so this session did not load the new file. No engine or adapter source file was saved to try it. The Hook triggers page also says File Save fires when the agent saves or modifies matching files. It is not documented whether a manual save in the editor fires it. Pending: open a new session, save a file under `taperescue/engine/`, and record the hook run here.
- The order-dependent property generator-coverage tests described above are a test-suite issue for a later task. They are not blocking the hook.
- Nothing was committed or pushed.

### Gaps found (not fixed, outside this task's files)
- **LGPL-2.1 text is not shipped.** The upstream gzstream files point to the LGPL but include no copy of it, and neither the repository nor the image holds one. The notices give the GNU URL. To meet section 6 strictly, add the text, for example as `LICENSES/LGPL-2.1.txt`, and copy it into the image.
- **The image carries no notices for the binaries or the bundle.** The `Dockerfile` copies `ScanTape`, `web/dist` and the server code into `/app`, but not `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` or `third_party/AtomTapeUtils/LICENSE.txt`. The MIT terms of AtomTapeUtils and React, and the LGPL notice for gzstream, require the notice to travel with distributed copies. A one-line `COPY LICENSE THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md third_party/AtomTapeUtils/LICENSE.txt /app/licenses/` would close this gap. The Python packages already carry their own license files in `dist-info`.
- **httpx, httpcore and certifi are test-only but ship in the image.** They come from the `server` extra. Moving `httpx` to the `dev` extra would remove three packages, including the only MPL-2.0 package, from the image. The notices list the image as it is today.
### Blockers
None.

Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue`. The image `taperescue:local` was rebuilt twice: once to read the new package list, and once more after `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` was updated, so that the image carries the final notices. Nothing was committed or pushed.
### Changes
- `LICENSES/LGPL-2.1.txt` (new): the official GNU LGPL-2.1 text, downloaded unchanged with `curl` from https://www.gnu.org/licenses/old-licenses/lgpl-2.1.txt.
- `Dockerfile`, runtime stage: copies `LICENSE` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` to `/app/licenses/`, `third_party/AtomTapeUtils/LICENSE.txt` to `/app/licenses/AtomTapeUtils-LICENSE.txt` (renamed so it does not sit next to TapeRescue's `LICENSE` under an ambiguous name), and `LICENSES/` to `/app/licenses/LICENSES/`.
- `pyproject.toml`: `httpx>=0.27` moved from the `server` extra to the `dev` extra, with a comment explaining that only the server tests use it.
- `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`:

  - The httpx and certifi notes were replaced by one note saying they are dev-only. MPL-2.0 was removed from the list of license texts in the image, and the LGPL-2.1 location was added.
  - httpx 0.28.1, httpcore 1.0.9 and certifi 2026.11.20 were added to the section 3 dev table.
- `README.md` line 39: the extras sentence now lists httpx under `dev`, not `server`. No other README line was touched.

## Task 25.2: Make the generator-coverage property tests independent of test collection
Requirements: 13.1, 13.5, 20.2
### Root cause
Hypothesis 6.170.17 mixes "local constants" into generation. `HypothesisProvider._maybe_draw_constant` (`hypothesis/internal/conjecture/providers.py`) replaces about 5% of primitive draws with a constant. The constant comes from either Hypothesis's built-in global pool or a local pool. `_get_local_constants()` builds the local pool from every module in `sys.modules` whose file `is_local_module_file` accepts: project files outside site-packages and not test files. Which `taperescue` modules are loaded depends on the test files pytest collected in the same process. Both coverage tests use `derandomize=True`, so their seed is fixed. A different pool still changes the drawn values and therefore the fixed sample.

Evidence 1, the pool differs by collection. A diagnostic pytest plugin outside the repo (`/tmp/tr_diag/constdiag.py`) printed the local modules and pool size before each coverage test:
| Collection | Local modules (besides the plugin and `/usr/lib/python3.12/sitecustomize.py`) | Pool |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `tests/engine tests/decoder_adapter tests/integration tests/properties` | 19 `taperescue` modules, including `taperescue.decoder_adapter.run`, `taperescue.decoder_adapter.schema`, `taperescue.fixtures.fsk` | 356 constants |
| `tests/properties` | 15 `taperescue` modules, without those three | 165 constants |

Evidence 2, removing the pool removes the failure. The same plugin with `TR_NO_LOCAL_CONSTANTS=1` replaced `_get_local_constants` with one returning an empty pool and cleared `CONSTANTS_CACHE`:
| Command | Pool as loaded | Pool emptied |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `pytest -p constdiag tests/engine tests/decoder_adapter tests/integration tests/properties -q` | `1 failed, 190 passed` (`test_generator_reaches_interesting_cases`) | `191 passed` |
| `pytest -p constdiag tests/engine tests/decoder_adapter tests/properties -q` | `160 passed` (the plugin's own constants changed the pool, so the 23.1 failure moved) | `160 passed` |

Evidence 3, after the fix the sample no longer depends on collection. A second plugin (`/tmp/tr_diag/samplediag.py`) hashed the `repr` of a 200-example derandomized sample of each file's `sessions()` strategy. It took one hash before fixture setup (pool as loaded) and one during the test call (pool pinned by the new fixture). Each run used `-k "generator_reaches or generator_covers"`.
| Collection | Pool | order_independence, as loaded / pinned | uef_roundtrip, as loaded / pinned |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| engine + decoder_adapter + integration + properties | 354 | `4da8426026fbb23d` / `6095b77d988315cc` | `a8cd01fe375a57e7` / `5a3e0954d7cb9e89` |
| engine + decoder_adapter + properties | 191 | `734e1db2c8428bb7` / `6095b77d988315cc` | `b3cc64702b86c418` / `5a3e0954d7cb9e89` |
| `tests/properties` | 163 | `734e1db2c8428bb7` / `6095b77d988315cc` | `41bd2af4fe1f469d` / `5a3e0954d7cb9e89` |
| the two files only | 142 | `7f8c86630158194f` / `6095b77d988315cc` | `a6c75e49cf4f33ff` / `5a3e0954d7cb9e89` |
| `tests` (full suite) | 868 | `4283babb96a05f27` / `6095b77d988315cc` | `e5256ff90b03a094` / `5a3e0954d7cb9e89` |

Hypothesis has no public setting or environment variable that turns local constants off. A `grep` of the installed package for `environ` and for `_get_local_constants` found none.

### Changes
- `tests/properties/conftest.py` (new). Fixture `fixed_constant_pool` (from line 31). It clears `providers.CONSTANTS_CACHE`, uses `monkeypatch` to replace `providers._get_local_constants` with a function returning an empty `Constants()`, and restores both afterwards. Hypothesis's global constants are still used. On a Hypothesis without local constants (`HypothesisProvider` has no `_local_constants`; `pyproject.toml` allows `hypothesis>=6.100`), the fixture does nothing. If a version has local constants but renames `_get_local_constants` or `CONSTANTS_CACHE`, the fixture calls `pytest.fail` with a message, so it never silently draws an unpinned sample.
- `tests/properties/test_prop_order_independence.py` lines 344-347: `test_generator_reaches_interesting_cases` takes the `fixed_constant_pool` fixture, and its docstring says why.
- `tests/properties/test_prop_uef_roundtrip.py` lines 349-352: `test_generator_covers_the_cases` takes the same fixture, with the same docstring note.
- Not changed: the thresholds, `max_examples`, `derandomize`, the strategies, every property test, the engine, and `.kiro/hooks/recovery-tests.json`. The property tests (`@SETTINGS @given(...)`) still run with the normal local pool, because the fixture applies only to the two coverage tests.

Alternatives considered: running each coverage sample in a subprocess (public API only, but the pool would still depend on that process's imports and environment, and it adds startup time per test); a plain-Python generator mirroring each strategy (the check would then test a copy, not the strategy the properties use); `@seed(...)` (does not help, because the seed is already fixed and the pool contents are what change).

### Commands and results
Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue`. Hypothesis 6.170.17. Decoder present at `build/decoder/ScanTape`.
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/engine tests/decoder_adapter tests/integration tests/properties -q`, before the fix | `1 failed, 190 passed in 42.89s`: `test_generator_reaches_interesting_cases`, `('status:Missing', ...) assert 4 >= 5` (reproduces 23.1) |
| Same command after the fix, run 1 | `191 passed in 39.43s` |
| Same command after the fix, run 2 | `191 passed in 40.01s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/properties/test_prop_order_independence.py -q` | `3 passed in 9.12s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/properties/test_prop_uef_roundtrip.py -q` | `3 passed in 15.17s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/engine tests/decoder_adapter tests/properties -q` (the other 23.1 failure) | `160 passed in 37.08s` |
| Hook command read from `.kiro/hooks/recovery-tests.json` and run with `bash -c`, `{"hook_event_name":"PostFileSave"}` on STDIN | Exit 0: `176 passed in 3.31s`, then `15 passed in 36.11s`, 40.4 s wall time |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `502 passed, 1 warning in 44.79s` |

After the older-Hypothesis guard was added to the fixture, every command was run again:
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Combined command, run 1 / run 2 | `191 passed in 29.25s` / `191 passed in 26.67s` |
| `test_prop_order_independence.py` alone / `test_prop_uef_roundtrip.py` alone | `3 passed in 6.45s` / `3 passed in 9.49s` |
| Hook command, as above | Exit 0: `176 passed in 2.14s`, then `15 passed in 25.26s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q` | `502 passed, 1 warning in 30.11s` |
| `samplediag` on `tests/properties` | Pinned hashes are unchanged: `6095b77d988315cc` and `5a3e0954d7cb9e89` |

### Note on the hook
The combined single call now passes, so the hook could use one pytest call: `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/engine tests/decoder_adapter tests/integration tests/properties -q`. As instructed, the hook file was not edited. Its description still says the coverage checks need a separate run, which is no longer true.

### Blockers
- None for this task. The fixture depends on private Hypothesis names (`HypothesisProvider._local_constants`, `providers._get_local_constants`, `providers.CONSTANTS_CACHE`). Only 6.170.17 was tested. The no-op path for older versions was not run.
- The diagnostic plugins are in `/tmp/tr_diag/`, outside the repo.
- Nothing was committed or pushed.

## Task 25.3: Create parent directories for CLI output paths

Requirements: 16.1, 16.3

Run from `/home/vamsik/TapeRescue` with Python 3.12.3 (`.venv`) and the decoder at `build/decoder/ScanTape`. Nothing was installed in the repository venv, committed or pushed. The temporary copy used for the README check (`/tmp/tr253.BNhs`) was deleted.

### Changes

- `taperescue/cli.py`: new `_ensure_parent(out)` creates the missing parent directories of an output file. A parent that exists as a regular file fails with `error: cannot create directory <dir>: it exists and is not a directory`; any other `OSError` (for example a file further up the path, `Not a directory`) fails with `error: cannot create directory <dir>: <strerror>`. Both exit 1 (`EXIT_FAILURE`); no exit code changed.
  - `_write_output` calls it, which covers `decode -o`, `recover --report` and the demo report.
  - `_export` calls it only when the gate permits export, before `export_uef`, so the exporter's temporary file (`tempfile.mkstemp(dir=out.parent)`) and the rename have a directory. A refused export creates no UEF directory.
  - `benchmark` calls it for `results.json` before `write_results` (which already ran `mkdir(parents=True)`), so a file in the way gives the same message as the other commands.
  - `demo` no longer creates `--out` eagerly before decoding. The directory is created when `report.json` is written, so a decoder failure leaves no empty directory behind.
  - `fixtures --out` was already handled by `generate()` (`out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`); unchanged.
  - Directories are created only after argument validation, `_check_outputs` (output equal to an input or to another output) and recovery. `_inputs_unchanged` is unchanged.
  - Module docstring documents the behaviour.
- `tests/test_cli.py`: new section "missing parent directories of output paths (task 25.3)", 12 test cases.
  - Without the decoder: `export -o x/y/out.uef` creates the dirs and leaves only the image (no temp file), and the image passes `verify-uef`; a refused `export` (exit 4) creates no directory; parent is a regular file (exit 1, "it exists and is not a directory") and a file further up (exit 1, "Not a directory"), with the file left unchanged; `recover --report new/r.json --uef a.wav` exits 2 and creates nothing; `fixtures --out x/y`.
  - With the decoder (`needs_decoder`, skipped when `build/decoder/ScanTape` is missing): `decode -o x/y/obs.json`; `decode -o file/obs.json` (exit 1); two-capture `recover --report r/s/report.json --uef u/v/STARMAP.uef` (exit 0, image verified, only the image in its dir); single-capture `recover` with the same paths exits 4, writes the report into the newly created `r/s/` and does not create `u/`; `demo --out x/y/demo`; `benchmark --out x/y` (Quadbike env vars unset).
- `README.md`: removed the `mkdir -p out` line from the step 5 "individual steps" block and replaced "The CLI does not create output directories:" with "The CLI creates missing output directories, such as `out/` below:". No other README line was changed by this task (the httpx wording change in step 1 visible in `git diff README.md` belongs to task 25.1).

### Test runs

| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_cli.py -q -rs` | `57 passed in 2.65s`, no skips |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/integration/test_end_to_end.py -q -rs` | `21 passed in 2.91s` |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -rs` | `502 passed, 1 warning in 47.32s`, no skips. The warning is Starlette's `httpx` deprecation notice. 502 = 490 (task 24) + 12 new cases |

## Task 25.4: Update design.md for the UEF carrier lengths
Requirements: 11.3
### Changes
- `.kiro/specs/tape-rescue/design.md`, UEF exporter "Output layout" list (lines 345-348):
  - Per-block carrier: "5100 cycles before block 0, 1800 cycles before other blocks" became "12240 cycles, 5.1 s, before block 0; 2160 cycles, 0.9 s, before other blocks".
  - Trailing carrier: now states 4800 cycles (2.0 s, the same as the fixture trailer).
  - New paragraph after the list: chunk `0x0110` is a 16-bit count of cycles at twice the base frequency (2400 Hz at 1200 Hz), citing fileformats.org, "UEF file format: Chunks" (https://fileformats.org/system/floppy/uef/chunks/), which cites the UEF 0.10 specification. It derives 5.1 × 2400 = 12240 and 0.9 × 2400 = 2160 and names the constants `FIRST_CARRIER_CYCLES`, `OTHER_CARRIER_CYCLES` and `TRAILING_CARRIER_CYCLES` in `taperescue/uef/exporter.py`.
- `.kiro/specs/tape-rescue/tasks.md`, task 9.4 exporter bullet (line 170) only: "carrier chunks (5100 cycles before block 0, 1800 before others, trailing tone)" became "carrier chunks in UEF chunk `0x0110` units of 2400 Hz cycles (12240 cycles = 5.1 s before block 0, 2160 = 0.9 s before others, 4800 = 2.0 s trailing tone)". No checkbox, task number or other task was changed.
- `.kiro/specs/tape-rescue/requirements.md`: no change. It has no match for `5100`, `1800`, `carrier` or `cycles`.
### Values checked against the code
`taperescue/uef/exporter.py`: `FIRST_CARRIER_CYCLES = 12240`, `OTHER_CARRIER_CYCLES = 2160`, `TRAILING_CARRIER_CYCLES = 4800`. The design's FSK section (line 441) already gave the same durations (5.1 s, 0.9 s, 2 s), so the two sections now agree.
### Commands and results
| Command | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `grep -nE '5100\|1800' design.md tasks.md requirements.md` | Only match: tasks.md line 437, the text of task 25.4 itself |
| `.venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/test_uef_exporter.py -q` | `13 passed in 0.36s` |
### Notes
- The remaining `5100/1800` in tasks.md is the description of task 25.4, which describes the change. I left it as written.
- Nothing was committed.
### Blockers
None.

## Task 26.1: Record the user's JSBeeb run of STARMAP (P1, P2)
Requirements: 22.2, 22.3
### What was supplied
The user was given these steps: start the container, click **Try sample**, click **Download tape image** (which saves `STARMAP.uef`, the image recovered from `damaged_a.wav` and `damaged_b.wav`), open JSBeeb at <https://bbc.xania.org/>, load the file with the local tape picker, type `*TAPE`, then `CHAIN "STARMAP"`. The user then posted one screenshot in the Kiro chat on 2026-11-23 (`date -I`) with the words "Here is the screenshot". The image is not in the repository yet. It is to be saved as `verification/evidence/jsbeeb-starmap.png`; `verification/evidence/README.md` records that it has not been added. The description of the screenshot below was supplied by the orchestrator, which could see it.

- JSBeeb UI: menus BBC B, Media, Reset, State, Debug, More. Status bar: "drive 0/2 Elite", "drive 1/3 empty", "cassette motor STARMAP", caps lock and shift lock LEDs, "virtual MHz".
- Emulated screen: title "STARMAP" at top centre; "The northern winter sky" (cyan); "240 stars plotted" (purple/blue); constellation labels Plough, Cassiopeia, Cygnus and Orion, each with cyan connecting lines between white "+" stars; a crescent moon top right; a scattered band of small blue/purple dots across the middle; a short white diagonal line top left; white "+" stars across the sky; purple hills along the bottom; the compass letters W, N and E along the bottom edge; a BASIC prompt `>_` at the top left.
- No error message ("Data?", "Block?", "Bad program", "No such variable" or any other) is visible.

Not shown and not stated by the user: which `.uef` file was loaded, its SHA-256, the tape load progress and the block-by-block loading messages, the browser used, and whether the web UI steps (strips, block inspector, the reconstructed-block confirmation dialog) were followed. "drive 0/2 Elite" is JSBeeb's default disc and plays no part in a cassette load. The "STARMAP" text beside the cassette indicator does not identify the file either: `STARMAP` is the cassette filename inside both the clean and the recovered image.
### Cross-check against `fixtures/starmap/STARMAP.bas`
Line offsets come from a throwaway script, `/tmp/t261_map.py`. It rebuilds the program with `build_program` from `STARMAP.bas`, asserts that it equals the concatenated `hex` payloads in `fixtures/starmap/reference_payloads.json` (it does: 2025 bytes, SHA-256 `4ac213a0…6e3a`), and prints `program_lines()` with the block each line occupies (block = offset // 256). The palette comes from lines 50 and 60: `VDU 19,1,4,0,0,0` maps logical colour 1 to blue and `VDU 19,2,6,0,0,0` maps logical colour 2 to cyan. Logical colour 3 keeps the MODE 1 default, white.

| Visible on screen | Source line(s) | Program offsets / block | Match |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| "STARMAP" at top centre | 1910 `COLOUR 3:PRINT TAB(16,0);"STARMAP"` (columns 16–22 of 40, row 0) | 1408–1433, block 5 | Yes |
| "The northern winter sky" in cyan | 1920 `COLOUR 2:PRINT TAB(7,1);"The northern winter sky"`; colour 2 is cyan (line 60) | 1434–1474, block 5 | Yes |
| "240 stars plotted" in purple/blue | 1930 `COLOUR 1:PRINT TAB(9,2);shown;" stars plotted"`; colour 1 is blue (line 50). `shown` is set to 0 at line 80 and incremented only at line 1090, once per star in `PROCstars(n)`, which line 100 calls with n=240 | 1475–1512, block 5; 165–175 and 194–208, block 0; 535–551, block 2 | Yes. 240 is the expected count |
| Labels Plough, Cassiopeia, Orion, Cygnus | 3070 `VDU 5:MOVE px+16,py-16:PRINT nm$:VDU 4` in `PROCconstellations`; names read from `DATA` lines 4010–4040; count from line 4000 `DATA 4` | 3070: 1649–1679, block 6. 4000: 1772–1778, block 6. 4010: 1779–1848, blocks 6–7. 4020–4040: 1849–2022, block 7 | Yes. All four names in the DATA lines are on screen, and there are no others |
| Cyan connecting lines | 3100 `GCOL 0,2:MOVE px,py:DRAW x,y` | 1702–1724, block 6 | Yes |
| White "+" stars at the constellation points | 3060 `GCOL 0,3:PROCbig(px,py)` and 3110 `GCOL 0,3:PROCbig(x,y)`; `PROCbig` (1210–1240) draws an 8-unit cross | 3060: 1628–1648, 3110: 1725–1743, block 6; `PROCbig`: 598–655, block 2 | Yes |
| White "+" stars across the sky | 1060 selects `GCOL 0,3` when b=10, and 1080 `IF b=10 THEN PROCbig(x,y)` | 460–534, blocks 1–2 | Yes |
| Band of small blue/purple dots across the middle | `PROCmilkyway(360)` (line 90; 1310–1390): `GCOL 0,1`, then `PLOT 69` points about `y=260+x*0.5`. Dim stars from `PROCstars` (b<6, `GCOL 0,1`) are also blue dots | blocks 2–3 | Yes. The description gives no slope or position, so the diagonal shape is not checked |
| Crescent moon top right | 110 `PROCmoon(1060,840,56,3)` (white disc), then 120 `PROCmoon(1084,858,50,0)` (black disc offset up and right), giving a crescent. `PROCmoon` is lines 1410–1500 | 209–256, blocks 0–1; 856–1036, blocks 3–4 | Yes. The description does not give the colour |
| Short white diagonal line top left | 130 `PROCmeteor(300,940,90)`; 1620 `GCOL 0,3:MOVE x,y:DRAW x-l,y-l/2` (white, from (300,940) to (210,895)) | 257–279, block 1; 1088–1114, block 4 | Yes. The cyan tail from line 1630 is not mentioned in the description and is not claimed |
| Purple hills along the bottom | `PROChorizon` (line 140; 1710–1790): `GCOL 0,1`, filled triangles (`PLOT 85`) up to `h=70+30*SIN(x/150)+RND(20)` | blocks 1, 4–5 | Yes, as a colour-naming difference only: the source uses logical colour 1 (blue). The same logical colour is used for "240 stars plotted" and the Milky Way dots, which the description calls purple/blue |
| W, N, E along the bottom edge | 1820–1840 `MOVE 16,40:PRINT "W"`, `MOVE 624,40:PRINT "N"`, `MOVE 1232,40:PRINT "E"` after `GCOL 0,3:VDU 5` (1810) | 1317–1383, block 5 | Yes: left, centre and right at y=40 |
| BASIC `>` prompt at top left, no error | 190 `END`. Line 1930's `PRINT` ends with a newline, so the text cursor is at row 3, column 0 when BASIC prints its prompt | 340–344, block 1 | Yes. The `_` after the prompt is not explained by the source: line 40 `VDU 23,1,0;0;0;0;` turns the text cursor off and the program never turns it back on. Whether BASIC II or the MOS shows a cursor again at the prompt was not checked. This does not affect the drawn content |

No mismatch was found between the screen content and the source.

`PROCconstellations` and block 6: `reference_payloads.json` gives `proc_constellations: {"block_no": 6, "first_offset": 1538, "last_offset": 1771}`, which agrees with task 6.8 and with the line table above (`DEF PROCconstellations` at 1538 through `ENDPROC` at 1767–1771). Block 6 spans program offsets 1536–1791. It also holds line 4000 `DATA 4` and the first 13 bytes of line 4010 (the Plough data). The labels, connecting lines and constellation crosses on screen are therefore produced by code that comes only from block 6, the block TapeRescue reconstructed.

What the run says about the four reconstructed bytes (offsets within block 6, values from `fixtures/starmap/damage.json`; script `/tmp/t261_dmg.py` printed the line and the bytes of each). The behaviour with each damaged value follows from the program text. Images containing the damaged values were not run in JSBeeb.

| Block 6 offset | Program offset | Line | Original (chosen) | Damaged value | Effect of the damaged value | Screenshot consistent with the chosen value? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 17 | 1553 | 3000 `DEF PROCconstellations` | 0x61 `a` | 0x65 `e` (A) | The definition becomes `DEF PROCconstelletions`. The call on line 150 finds no matching DEF, so BASIC stops with "No such FN/PROC" before any constellation is drawn | Yes: constellations drawn, no error |
| 64 | 1600 | 3040 `FOR k=1 TO c` (low byte of the line number) | 0xE0 (line 3040) | 0xE1 (B), which makes it line 3041 | Line 3041 still sorts between 3030 and 3050, and nothing refers to it by number, so the program would run the same | Cannot tell: the screen would look the same with either value |
| 143 | 1679 | 3070, the last byte, `4` of `VDU 4` | 0x34 `4` | 0x14 (A), a control code | The `VDU` argument is no longer a valid expression, so BASIC is expected to stop with an error the first time line 3070 runs, after the label "Plough" is printed. This was not tested, and the exact error message was not checked | Yes: all four labels drawn, no error |
| 201 | 1737 | 3110 `GCOL 0,3:PROCbig(x,y)` | 0x69 `i` | 0x29 `)` (B) | The call becomes `PROCb)g(x,y)`. There is no `DEF PROCb`, so BASIC stops with "No such FN/PROC" after the first connecting line | Yes: all constellations complete, no error |

So the screenshot is consistent with the chosen values at offsets 17, 143 and 201 and gives no information about offset 64. This is consistent with the CRC-matching reconstruction, but it does not prove every byte. The CRC match remains evidence, not proof of the original bytes.
